# API Reference — Cavnar AI

This documents **patterns and resource groups**, not every individual route — there are 1,249 URL rules (1,137 unique paths) across the app (`python3 scripts/repo_inventory.py`, 10/2/26 after the employee-app fix round), and the specific handler is always one `grep` away once you know which file and prefix to look in. That lookup is intentionally cheap; re-deriving the whole route table from scratch every session is not. Use this file to answer "which file, which blueprint, which auth" before opening anything.

## Blueprints (registered in `hosted_dashboard.py`)

| Blueprint | File | URL prefix | Auth | Routes (`python3 scripts/repo_inventory.py`, 10/2/26 after the employee-app fix round) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `client_bp` | `client_api.py` | `/api/*` (+ page and public-token routes: `/approve/<id>`, `/e/<token>`, `/s/<token>`, `/join/<token>`, `/u/<token>`, `/m/<token>.jpg`) | web session cookie (`auth.login_required`) | 242 |
| `mobile_bp` | `mobile_api.py` | `/mobile/api/*` | Bearer token (`auth.mobile_login_required`) | 252 |
| `strategy_bp` / `strategy_mobile_bp` | `strategy_routes.py` | each `_ROUTES` entry at `/api/…` **and** `/mobile/api/…` | session / bearer | 172 + 172 |
| `issue_link_bp` | `strategy_routes.py` | `/i/<token>` | signed token | 1 |
| `admin_bp` | `admin_routes.py` | `/admin/*`, plus `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/sms-optin-preview`, `/staff-sms-optin-preview` (the staff schedule-texts campaign's public opt-in; its label is `people.STAFF_SMS_CONSENT_TEXT`), `/.well-known/security.txt`, `/og-image.png`, `/favicon.*`, `/api/competitor-intel`, `/api/send-referral`, `/api/export-reviews`, `/api/log-activity` | `auth.admin_required` (writes need `is_admin`; the `support` role reads; the admin second factor, the request ceiling and, on sensitive routes, step-up — see *Admin console API*) | 185 |
| `audit_bp` | `sales_audit_routes.py` | `/admin/audits/*` (+ a public shared report by token) | admin | 22 |
| `staff_bp` | `staff_routes.py`, with routes added by `staff_account_routes.py`, `staff_device_routes.py`, `staff_comms_routes.py` and `staff_me_routes.py` (each imported by `staff_routes`) | `/staff/*` — PIN sign-in, the staff app's `/staff/api/*`, and the public calendar feed `/staff/cal/<token>.ics` | staff session (`auth.staff_login_required`: the `staff_session` cookie or `Authorization: Bearer`); sign-in, sign-up, forgot PIN, logout and the feed need none | 63 |
| `staff_knowledge_bp` | `staff_knowledge_routes.py` | `/staff/api/language`, `/staff/api/docs`, `/staff/api/ask` | staff session | 4 |
| `staff_comms_bp` / `staff_comms_mobile_bp` | `staff_comms_routes.py` | the Team inbox, `/api/labor/inbox*` and `/mobile/api/labor/inbox*` | session / bearer (CSRF on the web one) | 7 + 7 |
| `knowledge_bp` / `knowledge_mobile_bp` | `staff_knowledge_routes.py` | the lineup brief, house rules, staff docs and certifications, `/api/…` and `/mobile/api/…` | session / bearer (CSRF on the web one) | 13 + 13 |
| `auth_bp` | `auth_routes.py` | `/login`, `/logout`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password/<token>`, `/verify-2fa`, `/auth/*`, and the account-security `/api/*` routes (sessions, change-password, update-email, 2FA toggles) | none → issues session; the `/api/*` ones need a session | 31 |
| `webhook_bp` | `webhook_routes.py` | `/stripe-webhook`, `/webhooks/resend`, `/webhooks/twilio/sms`, `/webhooks/twilio/status`, `/docusign/callback`, `/docusign/callback2`, `/docusign/webhook` (inbound), and the public `/pay/<token>/<period>` | signature-verified (the pay link by its signed token) | 8 |
| `social_bp` | `social_routes.py` | Instagram/Facebook OAuth + `/api/post-to-facebook`, `/api/post-insights` | mixed | 9 |
| `toast_bp` / `square_bp` / `clover_bp` / `rpower_bp` | `*_routes.py` | POS connect + `/admin/<pos>/*` sync/bootstrap | mixed | 8 / 7 / 7 / 8 |
| `status_bp` | `status_routes.py` | `/status` public page, `/api/status`, `/admin/status/*` (update, incident, incident update / resolve, incidents, services), and `/admin/api/ops/state` (each system's state for the console) | public / `auth.admin_required`, as admin_bp | 9 |
| app-level | `hosted_dashboard.py` | `/` (+ `/schedule/studio`, the same shell), `/health` (read-only; the contract is in `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md` → Signals), `/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt`, `/og-image-v2.png`, `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` (+ `/apple-app-site-association`), and Flask's `/static/<path>` | — | 9 |

Every response carries `X-Request-ID` (an inbound `X-Request-ID` / `X-Railway-Request-Id` of 8–128 safe characters is echoed, else a new one) — quote it when reporting a failure; the logs are filterable by it. The outbound-webhook config routes (`GET/POST/DELETE /api/webhook`, `POST /api/webhook/test`) are in `client_bp`, not `webhook_bp`. `audit_app.py` is a separate standalone Flask app (the digital audit scorecard on :9000), not a blueprint. There is no web `/register` and no web `/me`; both exist only under `/mobile/api/` (and `/staff/api/me`). `hosted_dashboard` refuses to boot if two rules share a (path, method).

Two 409s a client may meet on writes. **`location_changed`**: the dashboard names the location a page was rendered for in `X-Cavnar-Restaurant-Id` (sent by `templates/_csrf_fetch.html` from the `cavnar-restaurant-id` meta tag); `auth.login_required` refuses a request whose id is not the session's current location, except `/api/switch-location` (DATA-10). A request without the header is unaffected. **Stale form**: a whole-form settings save (the phone profile save, both alert-settings saves) may carry `expected_version` — the `row_version` it loaded — and gets 409 with `current_version` if the row has moved on (`models.expected_version_from`, DATA-28); without it the save is last-write-wins as before.

## The `_m()` delegation pattern

59 of `client_bp`'s `/api/*` handlers are one line, and 100 are a bare `_m()` delegation once a docstring is set aside (counted 9/29/26; the rest own their body or share a `_do_*` with mobile — see *Which pattern to use* below):
```python
@client_bp.route("/api/ask-cavnar/opening")
@login_required
def ask_cavnar_opening(current_user):
    return _m("mobile_ask_opening")(current_user)
```
`_m(name)` looks up `mobile_api.<name>` and unwraps its `@mobile_login_required` decorator (`__wrapped__`), so the web route calls the exact same function body the iOS route calls — just with the already-resolved `current_user` from the web session instead of a decoded bearer token. **When a route's real logic isn't there, look in `mobile_api.py` first.** A genuinely web-only route (no iOS equivalent — e.g. CSV export, a desktop-only settings page) is one of the minority that doesn't delegate.

**Which pattern to use for a new endpoint both surfaces need.** Prefer a `strategy_routes._ROUTES` entry: one body, registered at both prefixes by the loop at the bottom of that file, with `_rid`/`_body`/`_principal` helpers. Second choice: a `_do_*` body in `client_api.py` called from both routes. Last: `_m()`. Never a third: 34 pairs still re-implement the same logic on both sides (885 lines, one of which has already diverged in its error handling), and each is a fix waiting to be missed on the other surface.

## Resource groups

### Account (`/api/account*`, `/mobile/api/account/*` — largest single group, ~36 mobile + ~26 client routes)
Profile, security (2FA setup/verify, backup codes, trusted devices, sessions list + revoke-others), team (invite/list/revoke — owner-role only), sign-in history, notification/alert-channel preferences, data export, billing status, marketing-email opt-out, timezone, connections status.

**Web/iOS account parity (9/25/26).**
- **Billing** — `GET /api/billing-info` and `GET /mobile/api/account/billing` are one body, `client_api._do_billing_info` (the `BILLING_PREVIEW_IDS` sample hook moved with it): `{ok, status, next_date, amount, interval, payment_method, portal_url, trial_end, invoices: [{date, amount, status, pdf_url}]}` — the web now has the last six invoices the app always had (Account → Billing → Recent invoices). Dates read M/D/YY (were M/D/YYYY). `{ok: false, reason: no_customer | no_key | stripe_error}` otherwise; a Stripe error's text is credential-redacted (`ai_guard.safe_error`) on both — the app's copy used to echo it raw. Scoped to the caller's restaurant's own `stripe_customer_id`. Since 9/29/26 every answer also carries `billing_status`, `pause_reason`, `paused_until` (M/D/YY) and `locked` (a hold the owner cannot resume); past-due, unpaid and paused subscriptions are listed, `portal_url` is returned whenever a Stripe customer exists, and a past-due account gets `past_due{hosted_invoice_url, amount_due, attempt_count, next_attempt}` and a `fix_url` (the invoice, else the card-update portal). A location covered by another location's subscription answers `{ok, status: "covered", billed_by{restaurant_id, name}, message, invoices: []}`. A login that is not the account holder gets 403 `{owner_only}`.
- **Email history** — `GET /api/email-history?limit=` (≤200): what was sent on the restaurant's behalf, each row with `sent_at` (UTC, what the phone parses), `sent_at_local` (the restaurant's own clock — what the web prints) and `label`; never a raw provider error.
- **Pause** — `GET /account/pause` (both prefixes) → `{ok, paused, paused_until, days, pause_reason, locked, lock_message, can_resume, can_pause}`; `POST /account/pause` `{days: 14|30|60}` → `{ok, paused_until, days}` (409 already paused or no subscription; 502 Stripe); `POST /account/resume` → `{ok}`, or **409 `{ok:false, locked:true, pause_reason, error}` for a hold** (a dispute, a refund or an admin pause — only an admin lifts it), 409 not paused, 502. (The web's billing-paused page does not yet know about holds: it offers Resume, which answers this 409.)
- **Close my account** — new web twin `POST /api/account/request-deletion` beside `POST /mobile/api/account/request-deletion`, one body (`client_api._do_request_account_deletion`): records `deletion_requested_at` for the caller's restaurant, emails Will once (not from a local backend — there the skip is recorded in the audit trail as `deletion.notice_skipped`), logs `deletion_requested`, and answers `{ok, requested_at, requested_on}` (`requested_on` is the date to show, M/D/YY on the restaurant's clock — the UTC stamp's own date reads a day late for an evening request); a repeat returns the first timestamp and sends nothing. Reachable with a lapsed subscription (billing-exempt, as the mobile route is). `GET /api/account-settings` adds `deletion_requested_at` (UTC stamp) and `deletion_requested_on` (M/D/YY, restaurant's clock) so the web card shows a request already on file. The web draws billing, Close my account, Manage alerts, the owner's email switches and the integration connect/disconnect buttons only for the account holder (`permissions.is_principal`, passed to the template as `is_principal`); another login sees a short owner-only note, and a `403 owner_only` from `/api/billing-info` reads as that note, never as "No active subscription". The web export picker lists `export_scopes` (`mobile_api.export_scopes_for`, the list iOS reads) and names any `refused` scope in its toast. The web card keeps the mailto to Will as a contact line.
- **Monthly review switch** — `GET /mobile/api/account` `account.monthly_review_enabled` (on unless off, as `/api/account-settings` reads it); iOS flips it through `POST /mobile/api/account/monthly-review` (`client_api._do_monthly_review_pref`, the web's `/api/account-settings/monthly-review` body).
- **iOS now uses** `morning-brief/settings` `briefing_level` (calm | normal | all — it always sends it), `issues/routing` (GET `{routing: {manager|escalation: {contact_id, name, phone, escalate_after_minutes}}, contacts: [{id, name, sms_consent}]}`; POST `{role, contact_id?}` — no `contact_id` clears) and `account/memory` + `account/memory/forget` — all existing `strategy_routes._ROUTES` twins (`account/memory` gained fields in the memory round, below).

**iOS parity round (10/7/26) — Account, settings and auth.**
- **Account health** — `GET /api/account/health` and `/mobile/api/account/health` (one body, `account_health.payload`) → `{ok, score, tone (good|warn|bad), sub, say: {lead, text}, fix: {key, label} | null, items: [{key, label, state (ok|warn|bad), sub, say}], connections: {rows, connected, total, pos_error}, checkup, features: [{key, label, detail, on}], measured: {line, net_monthly | null, evaluated, in_flight, measured}}`. The six rules the web scored in JS, now the server's; `subscription` is left out (and out of the score) for a login that isn't the account holder and reads the stored `billing_status`, never Stripe. `measured` is `value_delivered.delivered` only, viewer-scoped — never an opportunity, surfaced or avoided figure; `net_monthly` is null until something was measured.
- **Security checkup** — `GET /api/account/security-summary` and new twin `/mobile/api/account/security-summary` (one body, `client_api._do_security_summary`) add `checkup: {score, max: 100, items: [{key (two_fa|password|backup_codes|login_notify|recovery_email|devices), title, points, earned, detail, fix_label}]}` — the one scorer both clients draw (the app adds its own device-lock item outside the score).
- **Delete my login** — `POST /api/account/delete-login` and `/mobile/api/account/delete-login` (one body, `client_api._do_delete_own_login` → `auth.delete_own_login`): a teammate's own login turned off, its sessions, passkeys, remembered devices, push tokens and login prefs removed, and its email/username/phone/recovery/Apple/Google identifiers cleared (the address can be invited again). 403 for an account holder (Close my account is theirs) and in view-as; 400 for the restaurant's last login. Billing-exempt. Logged `login_deleted_by_holder`.
- **Staff sign-in notice** — new twin `POST /mobile/api/account/staff-signin-notify` `{enabled}` of `/api/toggle-staff-signin-notify` (one body, `client_api._do_staff_signin_notify`, owner-only) → `{ok, enabled}`; `GET /mobile/api/account` adds `account.staff_signin_notify`.
- **Team access** — `POST /mobile/api/account/team/<id>/access` answers `nightly_report` beside `access` and `morning_brief`.
- **Alert settings** — the switches one client lacked are written only when sent, on both saves (`client_api._alert_fields_when_sent`): `alert_rating_threshold`, `alert_rating_floor` (1–5, else 400), `alert_any_review`, `alert_resp_approved`, `alert_health_bypass_quiet`, `alert_food_waste`, `alert_ai_visibility_drop`, `alert_extra_emails`. `GET /api/alert-settings` adds the app's four; `GET /mobile/api/account` `alerts.settings` adds the web's four.
- **Account payload** — `GET /mobile/api/account` adds `reviews.auto_approve_4star` (so the app's save keeps it) and `connections.web_analytics: {connected, last_synced}`.

**Memory round (9/29/26) — Account.** Every route here is a `strategy_routes._ROUTES` entry at `/api/…` and `/mobile/api/…` unless it says otherwise.
- **Memory.** `GET account/memory[?archive_before=<id>]` (`owner_memory.account_view`) → `{ok, facts, lanes, others_private, archived, archive_more}`. `facts[]` are the facts THIS login may read: `{id, fact, kind (constraint|context|preference|goal|followup), source, created_at, created_on (M/D/YY), author, author_id, audience (team|principals|author), modules, subject, valid_until, valid_until_label, due_on, due_label, origin, can_forget, worded_by ("Cavnar AI" for a note Ask's remember tool wrote in its own words), confirmed_by, confirmed_on (another login said it again), pinned, can_pin (an account holder), author_left (its author no longer has access)}` — draw "Only owners" for `principals` and "Just you" for `author`, and Forget only when `can_forget`. `lanes[]` `{kind, count, cap}` — `count` is the facts THIS login may read (re-audit R3; it counted every row) — caps `models.ASK_MEMORY_CAPS`: 30 constraints, 30 context, 30 preferences, 10 goals, 20 follow-ups, split by whose it is (DATABASE_SCHEMA.md → `ask_memory`); a write past a budget moves that bucket's least-used fact to the archive. `others_private` (account holders): how many private notes other logins keep — a count, never the text. `archived[]` — what left without anyone asking — `{id, fact, kind, author, reason (evicted|expired|retracted|forgotten|replaced|contradicted|author_left), reason_label ("its lane was full", "its date passed", "its review date passed — put it back if it still holds" for a sales-audit observation, …), archived_on, can_restore}`, only those the login may read — filtered before the page (30) is cut; `archive_more` true means `?archive_before=<last id>` reads the next page. `POST account/memory/add` now takes `{fact (≤300), kind?, modules?: [..], valid_until?, due_on?, audience?}` (and `replaces?`: the id or exact text of a note this one replaces — archived as `replaced`) → `{ok, fact, kind, audience, evicted, evicted_facts, similar: [{id, fact, kind}], replaced, confirmed}` — `similar` are notes this login can read that look like the same thing or its opposite (ask "does this replace …?" and post again with `replaces`); `confirmed` means another login had already said it and this one was stamped on theirs; an unknown kind is `context`, an audience that is not one of the three takes the default (a follow-up is its author's; an account holder's fact about letting someone go, pay or a sale is `principals`), and a fact the store refuses (a measurable target in ANY kind — "set it as a goal") is a 400 with the reason. `POST account/memory/restore` `{id, valid_until?, due_on?}` → `{ok, fact, valid_until, due_on}` puts an archived fact back, read from the archive by id with this login's filter (`owner_memory.restore`) — 400 without a numeric id or with a past date, 404 when this login cannot read it, 403 unless `can_restore` (an account holder, or the fact's own author); a date that has passed is cleared (a follow-up is due in a week) unless a new one is sent, and a departed login's note comes back as the restoring account holder's, owner-only. `POST account/memory/dismiss` `{id}` → `{ok}` lets an archived fact go for good (same checks). `POST account/memory/pin` `{id, pinned?}` → `{ok, pinned}` — account holders only (403), at most 10 pinned per kind (400); a pinned fact is never evicted. Re-audit 9/29/26 (R6, PEOPLE-13): `facts[]` also carry `scope` (org | location), `from_location` (another location's organisation-wide fact — `can_forget` and `can_pin` false there) and `can_set_scope`; the payload has `can_set_org`. `POST account/memory/add` takes `scope: "org"` and `POST account/memory/scope` `{id, scope: org | location}` → `{ok, id, scope}` moves one of this location's facts to every location or back — a group owner only (403 otherwise, 400 without an id and scope, 404 for a fact not kept here). `add` also returns `private_default` (true when the fact went to `principals` because it is personnel or money — `owner_memory.is_private`, for ANY author since the re-audit, PEOPLE-10). `POST account/memory/audience` `{id, audience: team|principals|author}` → `{ok, audience}` — the one-tap "Share with the team" beside an owners-only fact: 404 for a fact this login may not read, 403 unless it is the fact's author or an account holder (only its author may make it `author`). Memory changes are logged to the activity log as their type and audience, never their words (`owner_memory.activity_detail`).
- **Preferences.** `GET account/preferences` → `{ok, mine, location, can_apply_to_all, locations: [{id, name}], org_keys, unmutable_types, alert_types: [{type, label}], never_opened}`. `mine` is this login's own choices (`preferences.LOGIN_KEYS`: `push_enabled`, `push_muted_types`, `quiet_start`, `quiet_end`) plus `morning_brief` (whether they get this location's brief). `location` is each shared setting (`voice_notes`, `never_say`, `sign_off_name`, `briefing_level`, `morning_brief_enabled`, `morning_brief_hour`, `alert_quiet_start`, `alert_quiet_end`, `alert_max_per_day`) as `{value, source: "all locations" | "this location" | "default", set_at}`. `alert_types` is the checklist both clients draw (every labelled notification type except `unmutable_types` — `health`, `issue`, `issue_escalated`, `coverage`, `critical_low`). `never_opened` rows are `{alert_type, delivered, opened, days: 60}`: pushes delivered to this login's devices at least 10 times in 60 days and never opened by them. `POST account/preferences/mine` `{push_enabled?, push_muted_types?, quiet_start?, quiet_end?, morning_brief?}` — any console login, for itself only → `{ok, mine}`; an unmutable type is dropped from the mute list, quiet hours are `HH:MM` (400 otherwise). These only ever take a push away from that login's phone: the location's alert matrix, quiet hours and cap apply first (`push.fire_push` then applies each login's own overrides). `POST account/preferences/apply-to-all` `{keys: [..]}` makes this location's values of `keys` (`preferences.ORG_KEYS` — voice, never-say, sign-off, brief, digest, quiet hours, the alert switches and channels) the organisation's default and every location's → `{ok, keys, locations, skipped}`; 403 unless an account holder with LOCATION_SWITCH on a location in a group (`preferences.may_apply_to_all`); 400 with `skipped` when none of the keys may be applied.
- **Engagement, per login.** `GET /api/notifications/engagement` and `/mobile/api/notifications/engagement` (one body, `client_api._do_notifications_engagement`) → `{ok, suggestions, mine}`: `suggestions` is the restaurant-wide list it always was; `mine` is `never_opened` above with a `label` each — "You never open 5★ alerts on your phone — mute them just for you?" — and its action is `POST account/preferences/mine`, never the restaurant's setting.
- **Change history.** `GET /api/account/activity` and `/mobile/api/account/activity` (one body) add `changes: [{kind, field, subject, old_value, new_value, source, who, changed_at, line}]` (`change_log.for_viewer`, newest first, at most 100): `line` is the sentence to show — "Labor target: 30 → 28, by the owner on 9/12/26". Pay changes only for an account holder; the operator's learning switches (`exclude_from_learning`, `learning_override`, `is_demo`) never.
- **Targets.** `GET account/targets` adds `labor: {goal}` and `food: {goal}` — the active goal the module judges against while it holds (`owner_memory.target_for`), `{pct, label, until (ISO), until_label (M/D/YY), goal_id}` or null ("Your goal of 26% by 12/31/26 applies") — and `set_notes: {field: "Set by the owner on 9/12/26"}` for each of `labor_target_pct`, `food_cost_target`, `waste_target_pct`, `monthly_revenue_target`, `weekly_revenue_target`, `hourly_rate` the change log has a change for.
- **Trust.** `GET account/trust` adds `lapsed: [{scope, subject, lapsed_on (M/D/YY), reason, text}]` (the automations whose trust lapsed and has not been earned back — the owner is asked again), `schedule.undone_on` (M/D/YY, the last undo of an auto-publish); each `auto_approve.bands` entry adds `retracted`, `weak_credit`, `autoposted_clean` and either `earned_at` or `lapsed: {at, reason}`; each supplier adds `undone_at` and `clean_since_undo` (an undo of an automatic order means `ordering.ORDER_TRUST_MIN` clean orders after it before the supplier sends on its own again).
- **Policy notice.** `/api/home/brief`, `/mobile/api/home` and the group brief carry `policy_notice` — `policy_notice.notice_for`: `{key, updated_on (ISO), updated_label (M/D/YY), text, link_label, url, shown_until, shown_until_label, dismiss: {web, mobile}}` or null. Only an account holder sees it (never an admin's view-as), for `policy_notice.NOTICE_DAYS` (30) days from `POLICY_UPDATED_ON` (None until the date is set at ship, so null everywhere today). `POST account/policy-notice/dismiss` → `{ok, dismissed}`; 403 for anyone but the account holder. A dismissal is per login and holds on every device and location until a later change.

### Labor (`/api/labor*`, `/mobile/api/labor/*` — ~27 mobile + ~17 client)

**Kitchen stations** (9/30/26): `GET /labor/rules` (both prefixes) carries `kitchen_stations: {roles, stations, needs, skills, active, roster_roles, kitchen_people}`; `POST /labor/rules {station_edit: {op, ...}}` (account holders) applies ONE change — `roles`, `add_station`, `remove_station`, `add_need`, `remove_need`, `skill` — against what is stored (`kitchen_stations.apply_edit`), 400 with the reason for a change that cannot apply. A generated week's `stations` (and `review.stations`) is `{stations, assigned: [{date, employee, shift_start, role, morning, night}], gaps: [{date, day, daypart, station}]}`.

**Scheduling notes held** (10/2/26): `GET /labor/staff-notes` parts carry `reading` (`{kind: hold|unchecked, why?, reason?, hold?: {days, dayparts, start, end, words}}`, `person_note_holds.read_part`) and `held` (`{id, words}`) when a hold was made from that note. `POST /labor/staff-note-holds {employee_name, part_text, days?, dayparts?, start?, end?}` (`_may_rate`, as the notes) stores one — the same person and words return the hold in force (`existing: true`); a day other than Monday–Sunday, a daypart other than morning/night, an end before the start or nothing to hold is a 400. `POST /labor/staff-note-holds/<id>/remove` releases it (404 when gone). Both write account activity.

**Schedule note rules** (10/1/26): `GET /labor/note-rules?week_start=` (both prefixes; anyone who sees labor) — `{sentences: [{text, kind: rule|unchecked|person|guidance, why, rule?: {role, role_choices, min, dayparts, days, scope}, rule_id?, enforced?}], rules: [{id, scope, week_start, role, min_people, dayparts, days, source_text, more_sources, words, stale?}], roles, week_of, can_edit}` for the saved notes (`schedule_note_rules.read_notes`, deterministic). `POST /labor/note-rules {role, min, dayparts, days?, scope: every|week, week_start?, source_text?}` (the account owner only, `_principal`) stores one confirmed minimum — the same rule twice returns the one in force (`existing: true`, the second sentence kept in `more_sources`); a day other than Monday–Sunday is a 400 — 400 with the reason for a role not on the roster, a count outside 1–10, no daypart, or a past week; `POST /labor/note-rules/<id>/remove` (owner only) retires one (404 when already gone). Both record an account activity event. Since the schedule audit of 10/3/26 (L-33) a rule has a `kind` — `floor` (the minimum above), or `start` / `end` with `at_time` ("4:30pm", `min_people` 0), made from a learned retime by "Make it a rule"; its `words` say which.

**Labor standards** (10/3/26, schedule audit D-25): `GET /labor/labor-standards` (both prefixes; anyone who sees labor) → `{standards: {family: {morning|night: {per_hour, unit, source: yours|measured, measured, days, text}}}, can_edit, families, units}` — per role family, the work one person carries an hour (guests per server-hour, bar tickets per bartender-hour, tickets per cook-hour) measured from the punches and `pos_tickets` (`labor_standards.standards`), the owner's own figure over it. `POST /labor/labor-standards` (the account owner only) `{family, lunch?, dinner?, all?}` sets one family's standard, `{family, remove: true}` clears it — read and written against what is stored, never a whole set sent back; 400 naming the bad entry; recorded in Account activity as `labor_standard_set`; → `{ok, standards}`. An owner's standard resizes that role's SHIFT REQUIREMENTS number with its reason.

**Schedule forecast** (10/1/26): `GET /labor/schedule-forecast?week_start=` (both prefixes; anyone who sees labor) — the Studio's Forecast tab before a draft (`schedule_engine.forecast_preview`, no model call). `week_start` is any date in the week, read as Generate reads it (400 with the reason for a past week or one over twelve weeks out); none is next week. `{available: false, reason}` without live shift history; else `{available: true, week_start, days: [{date, weekday, hours, closed, demand?, sales?, samples?, low?, high?, effects?, reason?}], projected_revenue, projected_revenue_source, hours_budget, labor_budget_dollars, labor_target, labor_target_label, hourly_rate, budget_basis, daily_target_basis, daily_target_reasons, demand_data_through}` — `hours` from `labor.week_hours_plan`, the same plan the draft is given. Since the schedule audit (10/3/26, workstream E): `labor_budget_dollars` is the HOURLY budget — the all-in target less the salaried staff's share of the week (D-1); `budget_basis` `{kind: all_in_less_salaries|all_in, target_pct, rate, rate_basis: measured|partly_assumed|assumed, assumed_share, caveat, trim_ok, text, closed_dates, closed_share, salaried_people?, trading_days?, salaries_exceed_target?, salaried_week_cost?, target_dollars?}` — the two dollar figures only for a reader who may see salaries (`models.viewer_sees_salaries`); `hourly_rate` is the measured wage (D-2) and `caveat` says when it rests on the assumed $26 (E-24); a day's `demand` `{pct, reasons, projected_sales, typical_sales}` is that date's one demand number (D-23/D-24/D-30) and `daily_target_reasons` why a day's target moved; `demand_data_through` `{date, days_ago, trading_days_ago, blind, stale, blocked, line, message}` — `blocked` is the state in which Generate refuses with `message` (D-33).
Shift CSV upload, labor % + ribbon data, schedule generation/history/sharing, staff availability, staff contacts (for schedule delivery), Operational Score (`team`), scheduling profiles (demand levels), overtime/overstaffed-day detail.

**Schedule generation, review and publish** (both prefixes unless noted; bodies in `strategy_routes._ROUTES` except the first four):
- `POST /api/generate-schedule` / `POST /mobile/api/labor/generate-schedule` — ONE body (POST only since 9/25/26: a GET is not guarded by CSRF or view-as-read-only and this starts a paid job; a GET is 405) (`mobile_api.mobile_generate_schedule`; the web route calls it, 9/25/26) → `{job_id}` (needs `SCHEDULE_DRAFT`); **429** `{ok:false, error}` when rate-limited on both (the web copy used to answer 200); `week_start` from the body or the query string; returns `joined: true` when a generation for this restaurant asked the same thing is already running — the same week, days, draft and instruction (`ops.running_job`, `schedule_engine.generation_request`) — poll that job; **409** `{ok:false, busy:true, running:{week_start, dates, history_id, instruction}, job_id, wait_seconds, typical, error}` (`job_id` is the run in the way, so a client can show that week being built and follow it — iOS parity 10/7/26) when a generation asked anything else is running ("A schedule for the week of 10/12/26 is being built — try again when it finishes. Nothing was started.", or "Cavnar AI is redoing Tuesday 10/13/26 of the week of 10/12/26 — …"): nothing starts and nothing is joined (re-audit 10/4/26 UI-8; it used to join any running generation and lose the owner's instruction). A redo saves only over the draft it read (PIPE-2): an edit saved to a kept day while it ran is taken into the redo (the job runs again from the model's answer, nothing paid twice); an edit to a day being redone keeps the owner's edit and the job ends in an error naming the day, nothing saved. A failure after the draft was saved finishes with that draft (`review.lines[0]` says the last step failed) or an error with `saved: true` and `history_id`, never "nothing was saved" (PIPE-8). A redo of some days sends `dates` + `history_id`, and optionally the owner's reason: a chip (`reason_chip` or `reason`, one of `schedule_engine.REDO_REASONS` — `too_few`/`too_thin`, `too_many`, `wrong_people`, `times`, `manager`, `overtime`, `fairness`, `other`; another value is kept as its own words) and their words (`reason_text` or `whats_wrong`, cut at 300 characters). The reason reaches every model call on the draft's `instruction` (`schedule_engine.redo_reason_from` / `with_redo_reason`, fenced, priority 5 — schedule audit 10/3/26 PR-18) and is recorded as the owner's rejection of those days (below). Every start answers `wait_seconds`: how long the job can run (its deadline plus the store's grace, P-22). The job runs on the bounded generation pool, not a thread of its own (P-39). `GET …/schedule-status/<job_id>` → `{status: "pending", seconds_left}` while it runs (`seconds_left` once the job has set its deadline; past it the job reads as an error, nothing saved), then the finished payload: `preview_rows` (rows may carry `needs_review`/`review_reason`), `summary` (deterministic diff lines against the last published week), `narrative` (the model's own note), `quality` (each `shifts[].assignments[]` carries a `why` explanation), `review` `{hard, soft, by_kind, lines, hard_rows, hard_days, fixes, unfixed, unmet, unwritten_dates, unstaffable_dates}` (since 10/3/26: `hard_rows` never holds the row a day-level breach — no manager on, a floor short — is pinned to; those are `hard_days` `[{date, day, kind, detail}]`, shown on the day; rule violations carry `day_level`) — `unmet` (schedule audit 10/3/26 PR-11) is everything the finished week does not meet, read from its rows by `schedule_output.unmet_items`: `[{kind (manager | floor | owner_rule | closer | close | role_close | coverage | leadership | strength | station | ask | min_hours | budget | unchecked_rule), date (ISO or ""), day, daypart (morning | night | ""), what, why, source, …}]`, owner-facing words with no ISO date, the manager minutes first —, `rule_violations`, `manager_coverage` `{extended, added, left: [{date, day, from, to, minutes, reasons: [{employee, why}], could_act}], shortfall: {unmanaged_hours, dates, text} | null}`, `min_hours` `{moved, added, left: [{employee, hours, min, short_by, reason}]}`, rows' `dst_hours` (+1.0 on the night the clocks go back: `scheduled_hours` is the real length), `pending_time_off`, `history_id`, `generation_seconds`, `chunked` (a bool since 10/3/26: more than one model call — the phone decodes it as Bool) with `calls` (how many), `roster`, `demand_by_date`, `learned_patterns`; since 10/3/26 also `partial` (days the generation could not write, the rest kept — P-34), `unwritten_dates` `[{date, day, why}]`, `unstaffable_dates` `[{date, day, reasons}]` (nobody on the team can work it — E-20), `starting_point` `{no_history, floors, borrowed}` (a first week with no history of its own — E-30), `regenerated_dates`, `redo` `{dates, base_history_id, by}` (a redo: the owner's or the quality gate's), `slices` (each call: `dates`, `part`, `rows`, `seconds`, `stop_reason`, `cut`, `partial_dates`, `model_call` — its tokens, rows and tokens a row — `off_list_dropped`, `pinned_dropped`, `error`). Since the repair loop (schedule audit 10/3/26, B1) also: `hours_hourly` / `hours_salaried` (the saved week split by pay, E-7, P-6), `repair` `{cycles, converged, restored_best, refused: [{stage, cycle, worse: [{label, tier, before, after}]}], skipped: [{stage, cycle, why}], budget: {hourly, budget, over_by, over, added_above_budget: {stage: hours}, trim_on, trim_ok, conflict}, hours_by_stage, seconds}` (P-47, E-18), `stage_failures` `[{stage, tier, blocks_publish, error, line}]` (a repair stage that raised, or `quality` — P-3, P-16, P-17), `stage_seconds` `{inputs, model, parse, rules, signals, repair, repair.<stage>…, sweep, score, save, total}` (P-24), `leader_rules_status` (F1's D-10 object), `end_overruns` `{padded, left}`; each `preview_rows[]` row carries `_rid`, its id from the parse to the save; `review` adds `stage_failures`, `input_problems` `[{source, name}]` (settings the rules could not read, each also a leading ⚠ line — P-1), `inputs_missing` (inputs the draft was made without, named in one line — P-17), `needs_review` `[{row_id, index, reason}]`, `repair`, `manager_coverage`, `min_hours`, `lead_lines`, `rule_lines`, `budget_lines`, and each `fixes[]` / `unfixed[]` entry carries its row's final `index`, `row_id` and (fixes) the `row` it describes (P-15); `gate` gains `triggers` `{date: [manager | rules | coverage | coverage_curve | leadership | operational_strength]}` when the quality gate rewrote days (P-43, SQ-20). An error's `error` names what stopped it — a paused AI budget, the provider down, data not current, a refusal, the time limit — never a bare "try again in a minute" (P-44).
- `GET /api/download-schedule` (`?history_id=` optional) — the schedule as CSV through the export writer: the week `history_id` names (this restaurant's only), else the newest draft nothing superseded — never a rewrite the quality gate discarded (`client_api._schedule_in_force`, P-42).
- `POST labor/schedule/score` `{rows, save?, history_id?}` → `{quality, violations, review}` — `review` carries `unmet` (what the rows as they stand miss, `schedule_output.week_review_extras`) and the week's `soft_requirements` re-read against them, both saved with an edit (schedule audit 10/3/26 PR-11); a save needs `SCHEDULE_DRAFT`, appends an `edited` version and writes `review_json` — the generation's review kept and only what the edited rows decide refreshed (`schedule_engine.refresh_review`, P-26: the counts and rule lines, `unfixed`, `needs_review`, the over-budget line, `manager_coverage.left` and `min_hours.left` still short, `unmet`; the fix lines kept and re-pointed at their rows, the trims, the stage failures, the rules it could not check and the setup kept; `edited: true`), and the response's `review` is that one. A sweep that cannot run leaves the stored review as it was. A save of a PUBLISHED week tells nobody (F2-2, 9/25/26): it answers `unsent_changes` — the names whose shifts differ from what each was last told (`schedule_versions.unsent_changes`: the last `published` version, or for the two people in a later cover/swap that `swap` version); null when the week was never sent — and `late_change_warning` — a string when shifts changed inside the owner's `notice_days` window ("…the restaurant may owe the affected staff premium pay… check with counsel"; never a sum), else null (NS5 H2). Whose each changed row is (schedule audit 10/3/26 L-5): a row may carry `origin` (`cavnar:apply_fixes` | `cavnar:optimize` | `cavnar:overtime_move`) and `origin_sig` — as apply-fixes and Improve hand them back; a flag whose row was changed again since is the manager's — and the body may carry `cavnar_changes: [{date, employee, shift_start, source}]` for rows Cavnar AI took out; Cavnar AI's own note tags are read too. The save stores it (`schedule_versions.row_origins_json`), credits the apply-fixes / Improve / overtime-move recommendation as implemented, and never teaches the manager's habits from it. A save of a sent week is observed as a reaction (L-4). It also answers `why_questions` (L-35): in a restaurant's first four weeks drafted with Cavnar AI and sent, up to three of the save's own big changes — `[{key, kind: moved_off | headcount_add | retime_start | retime_end | role_change, text, options: [{answer: always | this_week | call_off, label}], date, employee, role, daypart}]` (`call_off` only on a sent week, for a person taken off); `[]` otherwise.
- `POST labor/schedule/replacements` `{rows, index}` (`/api/labor/schedule/replacements`, `/mobile/api/labor/schedule/replacements`; one body, `mobile_api.mobile_schedule_replacements`) → `{ok, replacements: [{name, role, score, date, day}], employee, role}` — who could take row `index` instead of the person on it: the whole active roster in the shift's role (where their roles are known), each judged by `schedule_engine.replacement_is_legal` against one constraint set, the week swept once for every candidate (`prepare_replacements`, schedule audit 10/3/26 P-40) and breaches compared by identity, best rated first; 400 without rows and a valid index or past 2,000 rows.
- `POST labor/schedule/violations` `{rows, history_id?}` → `{violations, review, pending_time_off, hours}` — re-check after an edit; `review.unmet` as above. `hours` (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 SQ-5, `schedule_economics.hours_view`) is the Studio hours panel on the hourly basis the PAR and the daily targets are sized on: `{basis: "hourly", hourly, salaried, by_date, salaried_by_date, by_role, role_people, over: [{name, hours}], ceiling}` — salaried hours apart, never named by person. `POST labor/schedule/apply-fixes` `{rows}` → `{rows, fixes, unfixed, quality, review, cavnar_removed}` — puts somebody legal on every hard breach; the owner still saves. Each row it changed carries `origin: "cavnar:apply_fixes"` and `origin_sig`, and the proposal is presented as an `apply_fixes` recommendation (the save that keeps it implements it — L-5); `POST labor/schedule/optimize` does the same with `cavnar:optimize`, and both return `cavnar_removed` (rows taken out) for the save's `cavnar_changes`. A manager-plan row keeps its pin through both (re-audit 10/4/26 UI-6): `_pinned: "manager_plan"` / `_pin_reason` are read from the row a client sends or from the plan's mark in its notes ("Cavnar AI: manager plan — why", `schedule_skeleton.mark_pins`), no pass moves it, and the rows handed back carry them; `_rid` rides along. A save (`labor/schedule/score` with `save`) keeps the mark on the row that is still that manager's shift that day even when a client sent it without (`schedule_skeleton.carry_pins`). `POST labor/schedule/edit-why` `{history_id, key, answer: always | this_week | call_off}` (SCHEDULE_DRAFT, both prefixes) answers a save's `why_questions` (L-35): `always` makes the change a standing pattern at once with the answerer's authority (`source: owner_said`), `this_week` keeps it out of the learned habits, `call_off` records the person's call-out in attendance (source `manual`); an admin's answer through view-as is kept and counts once the account holder adopts it → `{ok, key, answer, counted, applied}`; 400 for an unknown question, an answer the question does not offer, or a different answer to one already answered.
- **The Shift Quality payload (`quality`, every schedule route above; schedule audit 10/3/26, additive)** — each `shifts[]` adds `no_shift_written` (a daypart the restaurant runs with nobody written onto it), `hard_breaches` (the hard rules broken on it, in the sweep's words; any one holds it at 50, `capped_by: "hard_rules"`), `profile.floors` (its critical floors) and per `dimensions[]` `floor`; every counting dimension is `customer_facing: true`. Leadership `facts.misses[]` add `why` (`nobody_on` / `not_qualified` / `short`), `on`, `credit`, `roles`; strength `facts.shortfalls[]`/`met[]` are `{role, strength (the rated people's average), average, bar, target, crew, crew_source, rated, on, roles}` and `facts.unrated`; demand match `facts` add `team_average`, `offset`, `by_role`; coverage by the hour `facts.floor_window`. `below_profile[]` add `capped_by`, `held_by`, `no_shift_written`, `reason`. The week adds `recommendation_details[]` (`{text, kind, points, dimension, date, daypart}`, aligned with `recommendations`) and `recommendation_points` (`{text: points}`); `confidence` adds `breakdown[]` (`{reason, points}`, biggest first) and `top_reason`. Shift profiles (`GET/POST labor/profiles`, web and mobile) carry `floors` (`{coverage|operational_strength|leadership|coverage_curve: 0-100}`, 0 = never caps).
- **Re-score, what-if, Improve and the solver (schedule audit 10/3/26, D2, additive)** — `POST labor/schedule/score` takes `what_if` (bool): the what-if (`shift_quality.compare_candidates`: same-role swaps and replacements, each held to every rule the week is — `what_if.checked_with` is `"every rule"`, or `"availability and hours"` where the week's rules could not be built) runs only when asked; without it `what_if` is `{ran: false, on_demand: true, reason}` (P-25). A re-score reuses the week's inputs, live check, rule sweep and exact scorer for 120 seconds per restaurant, `history_id`, dates, hour targets and capability version; a save rebuilds them. `quality.week_dimensions[]` may carry `learned` ("Learned patterns": what the week breaks of the scheduling memory — `facts.memories`, `broken`, `misses[{key, kind, weight, person, day, daypart, role, value, rows[{employee, date, shift_start, shift_end, role, daypart}], text}]`, `strained`; it withdraws with no active memory — L-3). The optimizer's `changes[]` (Improve, and `optimizer` on a generation) add `dollars` (what the change moved the week's labor dollars, overtime at its premium; null with no rate on file) and the kind `trade` (two people on one date trade shifts); the summary and `optimizer.solver` add `dollars_before` / `dollars_after` (P-32).
- `POST labor/publish-schedule` `{schedule_id, acknowledge?}` (needs `SCHEDULE_PUBLISH`). `schedule_id` is required (400 "Reload the schedule…" without it — it used to fall back to the newest row, maybe another week; F2-10). `acknowledge` is the list of blocker keys the client showed (`[key]`); a blocker not on it answers a new 409 (a bare `true` from an older client covers what the gate finds now; F2-9). The 409 is `{needs_ack, blockers[] (texts), blocker_keys[] (parallel), blocker_items[{key, text}], new_blockers[], notes[{key, text}]}` — `notes` are worth a look and never hold the send (10/3/26). On a week already sent with `unsent_changes`, Send tells only those people (`client_api.send_schedule_changes`: same permission, gate and `send_delay_minutes` window — queued as delayed kind `schedule_changes_send`, voided if the week is saved again inside it) and answers `{changes_sent: true, sent, unreachable, failed}`; with none it answers `already_published`. A staff email is `sent` only when `deliver()` says ok — a failed one is in `failed` and its share row removed (F2-1). `week_label` is M/D/YY. → `409 {needs_ack, …}` until a human acknowledges (`client_api.publish_blockers`: NEEDS REVIEW rows, hard breaches, a setting or source the rules could not read — `input:<source>:<name>`, "Settings for Ana couldn't be read — fix them in Team…" (schedule audit 10/3/26 P-1) — a week the quality engine could not check, with the cause (`quality_unchecked`, P-16), a repair stage of a hard rule or the manager rule that did not run on the draft (`stage:<stage>`, the review's `stage_failures` with `blocks_publish` — P-3, P-17), the notice rule, hourly hours past the hourly budget (`hours_over`, judged on the rows' hourly hours as they stand — salaried hours are never spent from it, E-7), and a change inside the notice window to a week staff already have (`late_change:<dates>`, E-22). Since 10/3/26 the quality verdict — weak week, low confidence, shifts below the bar — is a note (`notes`, `soft`), never a blocker (SQ-29)); on send stamps `published_at`/`published_by` and appends a `published` version. Friday auto-publish holds instead and tells the owner (`schedule_publish_held`).
  Since NS5 (9/24/26) the gate is `client_api.publish_review`, computed at send time: the rule sweep re-runs against today's data (time off approved after the draft, a floor, an age band) on top of the review saved with the week, plus `notice_short` against the notice rule (a week already under way says "started N days ago", never "starts in -N days"). A draft that would replace a week staff already have is checked instead with `schedule_rules.late_change_warning` over the dates whose shifts differ from it (`schedule_versions.shift_changes`), as are saved changes to a sent week before they are sent (E-22). With a send delay the queued row stores the blocker KEYS the person acknowledged (`acknowledge: [keys]`); when it runs, a blocker not on that list holds it — `409`-shaped result `{needs_ack, blockers, new_blockers}` — and the owner is told (`schedule_publish_held`). Auto-publish (and its delayed run, payload `{schedule_id, automatic: true}`) is held by any blocker and by the soft flags `schedule_rules.HOLD_UNATTENDED` (meal break, daily overtime, unanswered time off); other soft flags are named in the `schedule_publish_pending` notice.
- `GET labor/schedule-history/<id>`: each `preview_rows[]` row the manager plan placed carries `_pinned: "manager_plan"` and `_pin_reason`, read from the stored notes (re-audit 10/4/26 UI-6), so a reopened week shows its pins. It also returns `economics` (the stored `economics_json`, `{}` for a week built before 9/26/26; since 10/4/26 it keeps the `blended_rate` the week was priced at) and `projected_cost` — the rows priced as they stand now exactly as the generation priced them (`schedule_economics.week_pricing`: each person's rate, overtime from the 40h line per payroll week with the hours already published, salaried people free — hourly pay only) — and `labor_view` (below), so a reopened week states its labor % and overtime like a fresh one (Schedule Studio).
- `labor_view` (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 SQ-4, `schedule_economics.labor_view`; on the generation status poll once `done`, both twins, and on `labor/schedule-history/<id>`): the week's labor % on ONE stated basis, worked out for the reader — `{basis: "all_in" | "hourly", cost, hourly_cost, salaried_cost (owner only), pct, target_pct, target_basis, recent_pct, recent_days, savings, text}`. All-in for a reader who may see salaries (hourly pay plus the salaried staff's share of the week, against the all-in target and the all-in recent %); hourly for anyone else, against the hourly budget's share of sales. `savings` only when the recent % is on the same basis and above this week's — a projection, never earned. `projected_cost` alone is hourly pay.
- The page `GET /schedule/studio` is the dashboard opened on the Schedule Studio (same view as `/`; the template reads `request.path`).
- `GET labor/schedule-history/<id>/versions` → `{versions:[{version, reason: generated|edited|fixes|published, saved_by, changes, lines, score}], draft_vs_published}`.
- **The week's version travels with its rows (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 UI-1, UI-3, UI-4; additive).** `GET labor/schedule-history/<id>` adds `version` (the newest version, read in the same statement as the rows it returns) and `replaced_reason` (null, or why a copy a newer one replaced — `superseded_by` set — can't be edited or sent); the generation result adds `version` (the `generated` version its rows were stored as); a `POST labor/schedule/score` save adds `version` (the one it wrote); `POST labor/publish-schedule` and a send of changes add `version` (the `published` version written; on `already_published`, the newest). A save names the version its rows came from in `version`; it is refused (409 `{conflict, latest_version, saved_by, lines}`) only when the week's ROWS changed since that version (`schedule_versions.rows_changed_since`) — a Send's `published` version changes no row and is no conflict — or when it names none for a week that has versions; `saved_by`/`lines` name the newest version that changed rows, and a client keeping its edit saves again with `version: latest_version`. A save, publish, send of changes or delayed send of a replaced copy answers 409 `{replaced: true, superseded_by, error}`; `GET labor/publish-check` adds `superseded_by` and `replaced_reason`.
- Roster and rules: `GET labor/roster` (everyone incl. deactivated, with settings, score, reliability, pairs), `POST labor/staff-settings` (active, employment_type, min/max hours, per-day daypart availability, is_minor, `minor_age_band` `"14-15"|"16-17"|""` — NS5 H4), `POST labor/staff-pairs` / `DELETE labor/staff-pairs/<id>` (prefer/avoid), `GET/POST labor/rules` (compliance rules + per-role floors; `rules.keyholder_until_close` bool, default true), `GET/POST labor/demand-signals` + `DELETE labor/demand-signals/<id>` (events and reservation counts per date; GET without `start`/`end` reads from the restaurant's own today to 60 days after, never the server's UTC date, so tonight's game stays on the list after 7pm Central; POST takes rows, a `date,covers` CSV, or — since 10/3/26 (schedule audit D-31) — a reservation system's own booking export as `csv` (one row per booking with a date and a party size, columns found by their header words; cancelled and no-show bookings left out; summed per date into one `Reservations` row each, source `report`; the response adds `report: {dates, bookings, covers, skipped_status, skipped_unreadable, late_covers}`), recorded in Account activity as `demand_signals_saved` when it wrote a date; DELETE → `{ok, event_id}`, recorded in Account activity — `event_game_removed` for a game from a followed calendar, which stays off until put back, else `demand_signal_deleted`; a view-as removal names the admin; removing a past event, a catalog game or one the owner listed, re-measures that night at once — `engine.remeasure_past`, for a restaurant that learns for itself).
- `GET /mobile/api/labor/staff-contacts` (web twin `/api/labor/staff-contacts`) `?schedule_id=` → `{contacts:[{employee_name, email, phone, pos_id, channel: app|text|email|null}], schedule_id, week_start, week_end, reach (people.reach_summary), reachable}` — the people on the week named (404 for another restaurant's), else the newest week; `channel` is how Send reaches each one and `reachable` counts every channel (it counted email only, and listed the newest week's people while another week was being sent — 9/25/26).
- `GET labor/publish-check` `?schedule_id=` (both prefixes; `LABOR_VIEW`) → `{schedule_id, week_start, week_end, published_at, blockers[], blocker_keys[], blocker_items[{key, text}], notes[{key, text}], hours {hourly, salaried, total}, reach, texts_available, can_publish, unsent_changes}` (`notes`: the quality verdict, never a hold — SQ-29; `hours`: the week split by pay, E-7; a gate that could not run answers the blocker `check_failed`, never an empty list — P-16) (`unsent_changes`: null for an unsent week; for a sent one the names Send would tell, and `reach` counts only them) — what Send would do before it is pressed (Friction #4/#16). With no `schedule_id` it resolves the OPEN draft (newest unpublished, not superseded, week not ended, no published copy of the same week) or answers `schedule_id: null`. `reach` is `people.reach_summary`: `{total, reachable, by_app, by_text, by_email, unreachable[]}`. `likely_to_change` (schedule audit 10/3/26 L-15; null for a sent week): `{ready, reason, hit_rate, note, rows: [{employee, date, role, shift_start, likelihood, text}]}` — the rows the edit predictor flags, only once its backtest on this restaurant's own drafts is right 60% of the time over at least 4 held-out weeks (`ready: false` with the reason otherwise).
- Publishing reaches each person on their own channel (Friction #17): the app when their staff login has a registered device (`fire_push` narrowed to that login, `alert_type` `staff_schedule`), else a text when they ticked "text me when my schedule is posted" (`memberships.schedule_texts_at`) AND `TWILIO_STAFF_MESSAGING_SERVICE_SID` is set (`notify.send_sms(use_case="staff")` — never another campaign), else email. `sent[]` rows carry `channel: push|sms|email`; `unreachable[].reason` is "no app, text consent or email address on file". Since the employee-app fix round (10/2/26) the send goes through `people.deliver`, the one delivered-only rule: a push row says `delivery: "pending"` (queued; Apple has not answered yet) and its `sent_to: "app"` share row is written only once the push is delivered; a push no device took falls back to a consented text, then email; a text held overnight (10pm–8am restaurant time) says `delivery: "held_until_morning"`. A staff text needs the consent scope `schedule` (see *Staff app* below).
- People (Friction #25; `strategy_routes`, both prefixes): `GET people` (`LABOR_VIEW`) → `{people:[{key, name, role, active, has_login, phone, email}]}`; `GET people/<key>` → `{person:{key, name, role, job_title, active, has_login, membership_id, pin_set, phone, email, pos_id, hours{employment_type,min_hours,max_hours,desired_hours}, availability{dayparts,time_windows,preferred_dayparts,portal}, rating{score,notes,can_close}, certifications, is_minor, experienced, schedule_texts, pay_rate{role,rate,source}, choices, can_edit, can_manage_login}}`; `POST people/<key>` (`TEAM_RATE`) — a partial update, each field to the store that owns it (`people.update_person`): staff settings (`active`, hours, `daypart_availability`, `time_windows`, `certifications`, …), contacts (`email`, `phone`, `pos_id`), `rating` / `can_close` (capabilities), and — owner only (`TEAM_INVITE`) — `pin` / `job_title` on the staff login. `key` is the name slug ("Dana K." → `dana-k`), the id `person/<key>` nav paths carry.
- Staff availability (manager; both prefixes, one body in `mobile_api`, restaurant from the session — the active location for an owner who switched): `GET labor/availability` → `{availability:[{employee_name, available_days[], unavailable_days[], notes}]}` (day lists decoded); `POST labor/availability` `{employee_name, available_days[], unavailable_days?[], notes?}` — the employee rule (`staff_routes.availability_from_submission`): a day not ticked available is blocked, available is the complement of blocked, 7 of 7 blocked is 400, the note is trimmed to 300; `POST labor/availability/delete` `{employee_name}`. `/admin/staff-availability/<restaurant_id>` (GET/POST, `/delete`) is internal-admin only (`@admin_required`) since 9/25/26 — the web roster called it with `@login_required`, so any login could read or write another restaurant's rows.
- Shift requests (manager): `GET labor/shift-requests` → `{requests, open, offers}` (rows carry `kind` drop|swap|post and, for swaps, the target shift; `open` rows carry `offer_only`, `kind` and `decision_note`; `offers: [{id, request_id, name, status, note, issue_id, created_at, date, shift_start, shift_end, role, employee_name, kind}]` are shifts offered to one named person, still waiting on them); `POST labor/shift-requests/<id>/decide` `{decision: approve|deny, replacement?, note?}` (`SCHEDULE_DRAFT`) — approving a swap moves both shifts after the legality check runs both ways; `note` reaches the employee with the answer (`shift_change_requests.decision_note`); approving a drop that leaves its date with a stretch nobody manages adds `warning` ("Approving it leaves Saturday 10/10/26 5:00pm to 11:00pm with no manager on — name a manager to cover it"; `request.manager_gap` carries the same; schedule re-audit 10/4/26 RULES-15); 400 "that shift has already started" or "that shift is no longer on the schedule — the week changed …" (a denial is always allowed).
- **Open shifts and offers** (employee-app fix round, 10/2/26; `strategy_routes._ROUTES`, both prefixes, `SCHEDULE_DRAFT`, each written to Account activity): `POST labor/open-shifts` `{date, shift_start, employee?, shift_end?, role?, offer_to?, note?}` → `{ok, request, offer|null}` — a manager puts somebody's published shift (`employee`) or an extra one (`shift_end` + `role`; `employee_name` empty) on the open board, or with `offer_to` offers it to that one person in their app instead (`open_shift_posted`); `POST labor/shift-requests/<id>/offer` `{name, note?}` → `{ok, offer: {…, via: push|sms|email|app}}` — the person must pass the claim's own legality check and hold an active staff login (`open_shift_offered`); `POST labor/shift-requests/<id>/colleague-agreed` → `{ok, request}` — a swap colleague's yes given in person, so an approved swap goes ahead (`shift_swap_agreed_for`; 404 once the swap is not waiting on them); `POST labor/shift-requests/<id>/cancel` → `{ok, request}` — takes an open shift off the board and tells the holder, anyone it was offered to and the teammates told about it (`open_shift_cancelled`; 404 when it is not open). `POST issues/<id>/ask-cover` is now an in-app offer (`shift_requests.post_open_shift(..., offer_to=, issue_id=)`; the result gains `offer_id`, and `via` can be `app`), with the old text or email for someone with no staff login; the employee's answer in the app is written to the issue's `meta.asked[].answer`, and a yes covers that gap — the issue resolves once nobody on it is still missing. Since 10/3/26 (schedule audit E-4, E-31) a coverage issue is one per business date and role family (`coverage:<business date>:@<role>`): `meta.people[]` (each `{employee, role, shift_start, shift_end, minutes_late, status: missing|arrived|covered, hold, asked_off, notice_minutes}`) and `meta.covers[]` `{name, score, kind: stay|off, how, for, shift_start}` — each cover names the gap it is for, no name is suggested for two gaps, and someone on today whose shift ends as the gap begins (`kind: stay`) comes first. `ask-cover` offers the gap that cover was named for; `meta.asked[]` gains `for` and `shift_start`.
- **Floor sections** (V12, 10/2/26): `GET /api/labor/schedule/sections?start=&end=` and `/mobile/api/labor/schedule/sections` (one body in `mobile_api`) → `{ok, sections: [names in the owner's order], assigned: [{date, employee, shift_start: "HH:MM", section}], usual: [{employee, day, daypart, section, confidence, status, because}], foh_roles, can_edit}` (`can_edit`: the POST's own check, so the phone offers the names editor only where it saves; `usual`: each server's usual section from the scheduling memory, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-22); `POST` the same path (`SCHEDULE_DRAFT` or admin) with `{sections: [...]}` names them (`restaurants.foh_sections_json`) → `{ok, sections}`, or with `{date, employee, shift_start, section}` assigns one shift (`""` clears) → `{ok, section|null, sections}`; 400 for a section not on the list, a bad date, or more than 30 names ("You can name up to 30 sections."). The route takes the whole list: iOS re-reads it before each add or remove and sends it with the one change (FloorSectionsSheet). A section the owner removes from the list disappears everywhere.
- **Staff pulse** (V6, 10/2/26): `GET labor/staff-pulse?days=` (both prefixes, anyone who sees labor; default 14, at most 90) → `{ok, days, window, responses, min_responses: 3, enough, average, distribution, by_day: [{date, date_label, responses, average}], other_days_responses, notes: [{text, date_label|null}], message}` — how shifts felt to the staff, in aggregate only: below 3 answers a count and a message, `by_day` lists only days with 3 or more, and a note carries no name, role or id (its date only when its day cleared the floor). The close-out GET (`_do_closeout_get`) gains `staff_pulse: {responses, average, line}|null` (3 or more answers for the night), context only.
- Generation options: `POST generate-schedule` `{week_start?, dates?, history_id?, reason_chip?, reason_text?, instruction?}` — pick the week, or regenerate only some days of a draft with the rest pinned. A redo of some days is kept as the owner's rejection of those days with their optional reason (`reason_chip` or `reason`, `reason_text` or `whats_wrong`), and a whole week generated over a draft never sent as that draft discarded (`schedule_rejections`, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-26); `instruction` (schedule audit 10/3/26 PR-19, at most 500 characters) is what the owner asked for with this draft, handed to every model call of the generation at priority 5 (Ask Cavnar's `generate_schedule` card posts it, with the week, the days and the draft's `history_id`). Ask's `set_staff_hours` card posts `{employee_name, time_windows?, daypart_availability?}` — the person's whole stored maps with the new limit merged in — to `POST labor/staff-settings`. The finished payload also carries `trimmed`/`hours_trimmed` (the deterministic trim back to the budget: each entry is the shift as it was — `date, day, employee, role, shift_start, shift_end` — with `hours` taken, `reason`, and `kind` `"cut"` (ended early, `to` the new end; the close stagger goes before whole shifts) or `"removed"`; schedule audit 10/3/26 P-27), `review.budget_conflict` (`{over_by, budget, scheduled, held: {reason: shifts}, examples: [...]}` when the trim stopped above the budget because the shift requirements, a learned-worse day, a troubled daypart, a rule, a floor or minimum hours held the rest — said once beside the over-budget line, SQ-7), `review.cap_floor_conflicts` (`[{date, day, at, on, cap, held_by: [{kind: "floor", role, daypart, floor} | {kind: "rule", label}]}]` — nights left over the section count because a cut would go under a floor or break a rule, P-29), `staggered`, `projected_cost` (overtime at 1.5× past the ceiling), `over_budget_dollars`, `projected_revenue_source`, `hourly_profile_ready`, `demand_data_through`, `reservation_feed`, `holiday_lift`, `could_hold`, `departments`, `regenerated_dates`. Since the schedule audit (10/3/26, workstream E) it also carries `budget_basis` (what the hours budget is — the all-in target less the salaried share, or the whole target — the wage it is bought at, `caveat` when that wage is assumed and `trim_ok` false when too much of it is: never the salaries' dollars; D-1, D-2, E-24), `daily_target_basis` / `daily_target_reasons` (why a day's target moved, D-24), `date_demand` (`{date: {typical_sales, projected_sales, ratio, pct, reasons, sources, closed}}`, D-23/D-30), `requirements` (SHIFT REQUIREMENTS rows for the whole week, every role, each with `reasons` and `factor`, roles with `required`, `floor`, `typical`, `reason?`, `asked?`/`firm?`; a `late` row with its `window` on a night closing past 11pm, D-32) and `requirements_by_date` (`{"date|daypart": {role: people}}`, the firm numbers the score judges coverage against, P-19), `labor_standards` (D-25); `demand_data_through` is the freshness read the generation's gate made, with `line` for the banner and the summary (D-33). And `manager_plan` — the managers' shifts planned before the draft (`schedule_skeleton.payload`, schedule audit 10/3/26): `planned`, `failed`, `shifts` [{date, day, employee, role, shift_start, shift_end, hours, source (standing / usual / split / extended), reason}], `windows` {date: {from, to, source}}, `uncovered` [{date, day, from, to, minutes, why}], `unplanned` [{date, day, why}], `skipped` (standing shifts the rules refused, with why), `hours` {name: h}, `managers` [{name, role, salaried, pattern}], `acting` [{name, dates}], `unknown_pattern` [names] and `question` ("Which days and hours do … work?", or null); `review.manager_plan` keeps `question`, `unknown_pattern` and `uncovered` with the saved review. A row in `preview_rows` the plan placed carries `_pinned: "manager_plan"` and `_pin_reason`.
- Saves: `POST labor/schedule/score` takes `version`; a newer version on file returns `409 {conflict, latest_version, saved_by, lines}`. A save to a published week sends nothing and returns `unsent_changes`; Send (`labor/publish-schedule` on that week) tells them. `POST labor/schedule/violations` with `baseline_rows` adds `cost` (hours and overtime-priced dollars the edit moves) — priced as the generation prices the week (SQ-6): `{hours_before, hours_after, hours_delta (hourly hours), salaried_hours_delta, basis: "hourly", dollars_before, dollars_after, dollars_delta, overtime_hours_after}`.
- Rules (extended): `GET/POST labor/rules` also carry `jurisdiction` + `pack` (compliance_packs), `role_arrivals` (`{"Server": 5}` — whole minutes before each person's own shift start that role clocks in, 0..120, stored positive; GET also returns `roles_without_clock_in`, the roles whose people are all salaried), `role_close_mins` (the last of a role stays until N minutes after close), `manager_rule_unusable`, `role_requirements` (certifications by role), `foh_roles`, `patio_roles`, `role_cross_training` (whole percents per role; GET also returns `cross_training_defaults` per scheduled role and the flat `cross_training_default`), `trim_to_budget`, `floor_cap_conflicts` (GET, and POST when floors, the section count or the front-of-house roles change: `[{day, daypart, floor, cap, roles}]` — dayparts whose floors for the section-counted roles add up to more people than there are sections, which no draft can hold; schedule audit 10/3/26 P-29), `cut_floor_default` (whole number 1..10 — "never cut a role below N people", the cut floor for a role with no floor of its own; POST answers 400 on anything else and saves nothing; GET also returns `cut_floor_max`), `rules.manager_on_duty` and `manager_rule_unusable` (both always false since 10/2/26: the old per-daypart manager switch is superseded by the manager-every-minute rule, which is not a setting — `schedule_rules.compliance` forces it off), and the reservation provider (`reservation_provider`, `reservation_api_key`; `POST labor/reservations/sync`).
- **`POST labor/rules` is partial and all-or-nothing (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 UI-2, UI-9).** `rules` merges key by key into what is stored (`schedule_rules.merged_compliance`): a rule sent is set (null/blank is "not tracked"), a rule named in `rules_default: [keys]` goes back to its default (the pack's, else `DEFAULTS`), every other stored rule and the closures stay. Each per-role map — `role_floors`, `role_arrivals`, `role_requirements`, `role_cross_training`, `role_close_mins`/`role_close_stays` — merges role by role (`schedule_rules.merge_role_map`, case-insensitive): a role sent with a value is set, a role sent as null (or an empty spec / list) is removed, a role not sent is kept; a role's close stay writes both close settings, a role not sent keeps both. Every field is validated before anything is written and the save is one `update_restaurant`: a 400 stores nothing. `POST labor/time-off/<id>/decide`'s `request` carries `span_label`, as `GET labor/time-off` does (UI-10).
- Roster (extended): settings carry `time_windows`, `certifications`, `preferred_dayparts`, `desired_hours`; `GET labor/roster` adds `suggested_pairs`.
- **Managers, closers and roles (schedule audit 10/3/26 F1).** Each a `strategy_routes._ROUTES` entry at `/api/…` and `/mobile/api/…`.
  - `POST labor/staff-settings` also takes `floor_manager` (`true | false | "auto"`), `paid_hourly` (bool), `acting_manager` (`[{from, until, note?}]`, ISO or M/D/YY), `standing_shifts` (`[{day, start, end, role?}]`), `trainee` (`{target_role, trainer?, from?, until}`, `null`/`{}` ends it; the trainer must be on the roster) and `closes_for` (`[role]`); each replaces what is stored, 400 with the reason when it can't be read. `floor_manager`, `acting_manager` and `paid_hourly` are the account owner's (403 otherwise). `GET labor/roster` entries add `floor_manager: {counts, basis (set | set_not | role | held_role | recent_role | certificate | department | null), why, set}` and `can_close_pending` (a closer flag entered through support, not counted until the owner adopts it); `choices.certification_labels` (`manager` reads "Floor manager (can run the shift)"; `food_protection_manager` is its own) and `can_edit_owner_facts`.
  - `GET labor/managers` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, managers: [{name, role, basis, why, salaried, standing_shifts, acting_dates}], not_counted: [{name, role, basis, why}], acting: [{name, dates, label}], line ("Managers: Erik (Owner), …"), ask_standing ("Which days and hours do … work?" or null), missing_standing, week_dates, can_edit}` for next week. `POST labor/managers` `{name, floor_manager: true | false | "auto"}` (account owner; 400 for a name off the roster) → the same payload.
  - `GET labor/closers` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, by_role: [{role, family, closers}], flagged, roster, share, warning (past 30% of the roster marked, else null), closer_roles, outside_roles, pending_admin, suggestions: [{name, family, role, closes, action: keep | unmark | add, reason}], can_edit}` — the suggestions read the last 8 weeks of punches (who was the last of their role out) and change nothing. `POST labor/closers` `{changes: [{name, can_close: bool, closes_for?: [role]}], closer_roles?: [role]}` (account owner; each flag recorded as the owner's in `capability_changes`) → `{ok, applied, …the GET payload}`.
  - `GET labor/role-families` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, families: [{family, label, roles, source: yours | suggested}], stored, roles, merged, can_edit}` — every job code in use (roster, POS job list, punches) grouped into roles. `POST labor/role-families` `{families: {job code: role}}` (account owner; `{}` clears) → the same.
  - `GET labor/floors/suggest` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, floors: {role: {morning, night, days}}, current, weeks, percentile (25), dates, note, can_edit}` — "never below" per role and daypart from the 25th percentile of the last 8 weeks; saved only through `POST labor/rules {role_floors}`.
  - `GET labor/rules` adds `managers` (the `labor/managers` payload) and `managers_line`, `closers` (the `labor/closers` payload without suggestions), `owner_rules: [{text, reads_as}]`, `owner_rules_unchecked`, `hours_rules: [{text, reads_as}]` and `hours_rules_unchecked: [str]` (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 PROMPT-1: each line of the restaurant's hours & shift rules as every draft is checked against it, and the lines the schedule can't read), `close_times_missing` (trading weekdays with no close time), `role_families`, `closer_roles`, `salaried_cap` / `salaried_cap_default` (55) / `salaried_cap_bounds`, `certification_labels`, and `role_close_conflicts` (`[{role, stays, may_run}]`); `role_close_mins` is now the ONE "stays until close + N" per role (the must-stay and allowance merged). `POST labor/rules` takes `role_close_mins` (or `role_close_stays`) — written to both stored settings — `salaried_cap` (20-84, blank for the default; 400 otherwise) and `closer_roles` (`[role]`).
  - `POST labor/team/thresholds` (and `/api/labor/team/thresholds`): each `leader_rules` entry is read the way the engine reads it (`schedule_setup.clean_leader_rules`: role, count 1-10, days, daypart morning/night, min_score 1-5, attribute `can_close`, closing) — 400 with the reasons when one can't be — and checked against the team: the answer adds `leader_rule_warnings` ("Only 1 Bartender AM scores 5 or above, so this rule can't be met on 6 of its 7 shifts"), also folded into `warnings`. `GET labor/team` adds `leader_rule_defaults` (`{min_score: 4, count: 1}`), `leader_rules_status` (`{rules, score_rules, attribute_rules, rated, admin_ratings, closers, admin_closers, active, can_adopt, lines}`) and per person `can_close_pending`.
  - `POST account/memory/add` returns `schedule_rule: {checked, reads_as, text}` for an account holder's staffing rule ("Checked on every draft as: at least 2 Server PM on Sat at dinner/night"), null otherwise.
  - A generated week's `review` adds `setup: [{kind, text, line, …}]` (kinds `managers`, `managers_unconfirmed`, `acting`, `standing_missing`, `closers_share`, `close_times_missing`, `leader_rules_inactive` with `can_adopt`, `owner_rule`, `trainee`, `owner_salaried`); every item with `line` true is also in `review.lines`. The result carries `leader_rules_status`. New breaches: `role_not_held` (hard, a person-level rule), `closer_unavailable` and `trainee_unpaired` (soft); `keyholder_until_close` now carries `close_role` (the role family) and `day_level`.
- `GET labor/learned-patterns` / `POST labor/learned-patterns` `{key, dismissed}`, `{key, rule: true}` or `{key, keep: true|false}` — the owner's say over what the draft learns (standing patterns, conflicts and rules: the memory round, below). `POST labor/schedule/recommendation` `{kind, key, action}` — the accept/dismiss ledger. `GET labor/intel` — outcomes by daypart, the rotation ledger, behaviour-learned preferences, who could hold a station, suggested pairs, sales per labor hour, the revenue projection; `edit_prediction` (ready or why not, and its held-out hit rate on past drafts), `weight_calibration` (per dimension: current, suggested, step, the driving outcome and an `explanation`; since the schedule fix round, 10/3/26 SQ-22, also `profiles` — per shift profile `{label, shifts, ready, explanation | bar: {current, suggested, fitted, evidence, below, above, shifts, explanation}, floors: {dimension: same}}`, read only from `CALIBRATION_MIN_PROFILE_SHIFTS` (30) of the profile's own shifts — `suggested_profiles` `{key: {min_quality?, floors?}}`, `moving_profiles`, and `fit[outcome].left_out` (dimensions the record cannot carry at 10 shifts each); `POST labor/quality/calibration/apply` writes the floors and bars too and returns `profiles` beside `weights`), `rotation` (the multi-week plan and its `lines`), `splh_objective` (the targets and their basis) and `starting_points` (a borrowed starting headcount for a restaurant with no history, or the reason there is none). A generated schedule also carries `likely_edits` (predicted rows carry `likelihood` and `reason`), `rotation_plan`, `splh_objective`, `splh_report` and `starting_headcount`.

**Memory round (9/29/26) — Labor.** Each route is a `strategy_routes._ROUTES` entry at `/api/…` and `/mobile/api/…` unless it says otherwise.
- **Dated scheduling notes** (the constraints the draft and the labor read obey, which only an admin could see before). `GET labor/staff-notes` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, notes: [{id, employee_name, notes, noted (M/D/YY), noted_on (ISO), stale, expires_on, parts: [{index, text, noted, noted_on, expires, expires_on, ended, stale}]}], stale_after_days (models.STAFF_NOTE_STALE_DAYS, 90), stale (how many parts are), can_edit}` — ended parts included, to grey out; a stale part asks "Still true?". `POST labor/staff-notes` `{employee_name, notes (≤500), expires_on? (M/D/YY or ISO)}` (TEAM_RATE) adds a constraint to that person's note, dated today; an end in its own words ("out until 6/1") retires it too → `{ok, appended, note}`; 400 without a name and a constraint, or with an end that is not a date. `POST labor/staff-notes/<id>` `{action: confirm | expire | remove, part?, expires_on?}` (TEAM_RATE) — "still true", "ends on", "gone"; `part` is the constraint's index, none means every one on the note → `{ok, note (null once removed), removed}`; 404 for another restaurant's. Adds, ends and removals are `change_log` roster rows for that person. The action queue carries `staff_note:stale` ("N scheduling notes over 90 days old — still true?", nav `labor/notes`).
- **Who is who** (`people.py`; nothing is ever merged on a guess). `GET people/identity` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, questions: [{id, kind, reason, a: {person_id, name, key, shifts, rated, sources}, b: {…}, asked}], can_answer}` — two records that may be one person ("Kim T." / "Kim Tran"), or two people the POS spells alike. `POST people/identity/<id>` `{same: bool, keep?: person_id}` (account holder only) → `{ok, status: merged | different, …}` — "Same person" merges (into `keep`, else the one with the POS id or the older record) and "Different people" closes the question for good; 400 without a boolean, 409 when it cannot be done. `POST people/merge` `{from, into}` (two person keys; account holder only) → `{ok, into, from, into_id, from_id, moved: {table: rows}, folded}` — every rating, setting, note, availability, time off, contact, pairing, request, staff login and shift follows; 400 unless both are on the list, 409 when a key names two people. `POST people/<key>/rename` `{name}` (account holder only) → `{ok, from, to, key}`; the old spelling stays an alias, so a later upload under it still finds the person; 404 for a key not on the roster, 409 when the new name is somebody else's (that is a merge). Re-audit 9/29/26: `people/merge` also returns `{merge_id, undo_days}`; `GET people/merges` (LABOR_VIEW) → `{ok, merges: [{merge_id, from, into, merged_on, undo_until (M/D/YY), undoable, why_not, undone}], can_undo, undo_days}` — the last 30 days; `POST people/merges/<id>/undo` (account holder only) → `{ok, from, into, restored: {table: rows}}` (`people.unmerge_people`), 409 when already undone, older than 30 days, or either person was renamed or merged since; `POST people/<key>/erase` `{confirm: their name}` (account holder only) → `{ok, erased: {table: rows}}` (`people.erase_person`) — 400 unless the name is typed back, 409 while they are on the roster or hold a staff login, 404 for a key not on the roster.
