  - `recheck_on`, `recheck_verdict` (`held` | `faded` | `reversed` | `unknown` | null), `recheck_value`, `rechecked_at`, `validated` (bool: improved, held at re-check, nothing else changed on its family), `counts` (still counted in Delivered), `result_line` (`"Average rating 4.2★ → 4.4★, improved"`), `window_days`.
  - `status=tracking` rows add `interim` `{value, delta, delta_pct, as_of, days_in, verdict, baseline, band, multiple}` (also `days`, = `days_in`) — a partial reading, labelled as such.
  - Calibration (confidence audit 9/24/26): `baseline_kind` may also be `before the trigger`; `trigger_start` / `trigger_end` / `trigger_value` (the window that fired the recommendation, ISO; null = not triggered); `baseline_overlaps_trigger` (bool — shown, `counts` false, never a win or a loss); `noise_band` (this restaurant's band in the metric's unit, null = the stated band), `noise_sigma`, `false_alarm_rate` (two-sided), `band_basis` (words); `grade_phrase` — the grade as one clause ("held" only for `held`). A check-in with `conditions_changed` now sets `counts` false, as "no" does. Advice not taken (`observed:untaken:*`) is never listed; `observed:supplier_order_sent:*` rows are `informational`.
- **`GET value`** → `{ok, delivered, avoided, opportunity, surfaced, ledger, promise?, rates}` — the four figures still never summed. Everything this login may not see (`value_delivered.viewer_scope`: denied modules, comps/voids without LOSS_VIEW, results whose recommendation it may not see) is dropped BEFORE `total_value`, `best_ever`, `cumulative` and `unpriced_wins` (re-audit A26). `delivered` adds:
  - `worsened` `{count, monthly, priced_count}` and `net_monthly` (= `monthly` − `worsened.monthly`; may be negative, and `net_note` says so), `net_by_module`. `monthly` stays the improvements alone. `count` includes unpriced results (a rating that fell); `priced_count` is how many make up `worsened.monthly` — "less $X from N that got worse" uses `priced_count`.
  - `annual` is `monthly` × 12 and `annual_basis` says so: a projection, not a measurement.
  - **Savings only (re-audit A6).** `monthly`, `worsened`, `net_monthly`, `by_module` and `biggest` are cost savings (labor, food cost, comps, voids, waste, overtime). A sales rise is gross revenue — money through the till, not profit — and is reported apart in `sales_lift` `{monthly, wins, worsened: {count, monthly}, net_monthly, by_module, basis}`, never added to the savings; `sales_pricing` is `"separate"`. A labor/food-cost/comp/void move over the same weeks as a sales move the same way is not counted as a saving (its share of sales fell because sales rose; re-audit A5). Within one family a narrower reading overlapping a broader one (overtime inside labor %, waste inside food cost %, a weekday inside sales, complaints inside the rating) is the broader one's — wins and losses netted per family before anything is summed (A7) — and of the rest, the non-overlapping set of largest dollars counts (A20).
  - Monthly dollars for a share of sales (labor %, food cost %, comps, voids, sales) are priced on the restaurant's own **trading days** (`metrics.days_per_month`: the weekdays it trades × 52/12 — 26 for a restaurant closed Mondays), and accrue only on days actually traded and measured (re-audit A1).
  - `validated_monthly`, `validated` (count), `faded` (wins that no longer held at re-check — no longer in `monthly`).
  - `consistent_monthly` / `consistent` (improvements that were a clear move or held) and `associated_monthly` / `associated` (tied to other changes, or past the band once) — the two parts of `monthly` by grade, quoted apart, never added to anything again; `cumulative.by_grade` `{consistent_or_held, associated}` is the same split of the measured days. The Home `value` headline carries `consistent_monthly` / `associated_monthly` too.
  - `wins_measured` (distinct improvements, priced or not), `wins_by_module` `{module: count}`, `unpriced_wins` `[{title, module, line}]` (rating and reply-time wins: counted, never priced).
  - `cumulative` `{total, gained, lost, since, until, days, measured_days, by_module, sales_lift, basis}` — a sum of measured days (`outcome_value_days`, summed in SQL: one reading per family and day, the broadest that held, then the largest; disowned trackers excluded), net of changes that got worse; `total` is null when no day has been measured. `sales_lift` is the same sum for sales readings (`{total, gained, lost, since, until, days, measured_days, by_module, basis}`, null when none), never added to `total`.
  - `rates` — the same object as the top-level `rates`: `{reply_rate, reply_rate_basis, reply_minutes, agency_monthly, agency_basis, schedule_minutes, invoice_minutes, overtime_multiplier, overtime_threshold_hours, overtime_basis, days_per_month, weeks_per_month, recheck_days, accrual_horizon_days, consistent_multiple}`. A surface pricing replies reads `rates.reply_rate`, never its own 5.
  - `ledger` adds `outcomes_improved` and its line.
- **Tracker-start replies.** Every door that can start a tracker answers with exactly one of:
  - `tracker` `{id, metric, label, evaluate_on, window_days, baseline_kind, module, label_text}` — `label_text` e.g. `"measuring labor % until 10/21/26"`;
  - `tracker_refused` `{code, reason, in_flight_until, in_flight?}` — `code` `in_flight` (with `in_flight` `{id, metric, label, title}` and `reason` e.g. `"Already measuring labor % until 10/21/26. One change per number at a time…"`), `no_metric` (`"There is nothing here Cavnar can measure it against yet"`) or `not_measurable`.
  
  Where: `POST outcomes` (Home Track; `ok: true` with `tracker_refused`, `warning` = `message` = its reason, no `outcome` — the recommendation is still recorded as accepted); `POST recs/event` for `accepted` (always one of the two) and `completed` (only when the recommendation carries a metric — Done without one promises nothing; never on an episode already answered — a replayed answer is recorded, starts nothing and says `already_answered: true`), plus the existing `message` / `tracking`; `POST /api/home/dismiss` + `/mobile/api/home/dismiss` with `kind: done` (keeps `outcome: {id, evaluate_on}` when started); `POST labor/schedule/recommendation` (`kind: hours`, accepted); `POST food-cost/reprice/apply` and `POST food-cost/menu-item-price` (when the price follows a suggestion). A slow-day campaign's tracker starts on a background thread after the sends and has no reply to carry.
  
  Gates: an owner-pressed Track and Ask's `track_outcome` refuse while another tracker measures the **same metric** (keys normalised: `weekday_sales:tuesday` is `weekday_sales:Tuesday`); every automatic start — observed actions (a schedule published, an order sent; at most once per metric per calendar month, whatever became of the first), reprice, campaigns, schedule accepts, `recs/event` Accept/Done and Home Done — refuses while anything in the **same family** is measured (`outcomes.AUTOMATIC_SOURCES`, re-audit A18). An alert-opened tracker never blocks a real one. Two starts at once are serialised (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`): the second is answered by the first, never a 500. Dates (`started_on`, `evaluate_on`) are the restaurant's own calendar date, not the server's.

  `POST outcomes` with a `source_key` (Track this on a recommendation — contract K2): 404 `{ok: false, error: "Recommendation not found."}` unless the key's latest episode exists, was shown, and this login may see it. What the recommendation carries wins over the body's `metric`: a DSR action by its kind (`dsr_action:reorder:…` answers `tracker_refused` `no_metric`), a slow day by that weekday's sales, an overtime card or move by `overtime_hours`. A tracker already running on the key is returned only when it measures the same number and this login may see it — otherwise 409 `{ok: false, error: "Something else is already being measured for this."}`, its details withheld. A metric the login may not see is 403. Taking it silences the card for max(14 days, the tracker's window). `POST outcomes/<id>/abandon` answers `{ok: true}` whether or not anything changed: a tracker of another restaurant, or one this login may not see, is left alone (re-audit A28).
- **`GET metrics`** also lists `overtime_hours` (hours past 40 per payroll week; dollars at the overtime premium) and `response_hours` (median hours from review to reply; no dollars) — both accepted by `POST goals`, `POST outcomes` and Ask.

### Webhooks (`/api/webhook*` outbound config, `/webhooks/*` inbound)
`GET /api/webhook` (current config or null), `POST /api/webhook` (save + return a secret), `POST /api/webhook/test` (the account holder only, 403 `owner_only`; QUEUED on the delivery pool through `net_safety`, never fired inline → `{ok, queued: true, event_id, message}`; `{ok:false, error}` with no webhook configured), `DELETE /api/webhook` (remove). Every outbound delivery carries an event `id` in the payload and in `X-Cavnar-Event-Id`, so a receiver can drop a redelivery; the URL must resolve to a public address and redirects are not followed.

Inbound, each signature-verified before anything runs and each failure counted per provider (`webhook_verifications` for Stripe and DocuSign, `inbound_webhook_health` for Resend and Twilio):
- `POST /stripe-webhook` — the secret is read per request: unset → 401 `{error:"Unauthorized"}`; bad signature → 400; a duplicate event → 200 `{received:true, duplicate:true}`; a handler failure → 500 with the claim released, so Stripe redelivers. Handled: `checkout.session.completed`, `customer.subscription.created` / `updated` / `deleted`, `invoice.paid`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `invoice.payment_action_required`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` / `closed`; anything else is recorded and answered 200.
- `POST /docusign/webhook` — HMAC; 401 without the secret or with a bad signature; completed, declined, voided and delivered envelopes (declined / voided / delivered update `contract_status` only for the restaurant's current envelope, never over `signed`).
- `POST /webhooks/resend` — Svix signature (403 on failure); bounces and complaints suppress with a scope; delivery events move `email_log` forward only.
- `POST /webhooks/twilio/sms` (inbound texts — STOP and the text club's replies, `guest_marketing.handle_inbound_sms`) and **`POST /webhooks/twilio/status`** (delivery callbacks every text asks for) — Twilio signature over the rebuilt https URL, 403 when it fails or the token is unset; the status callback answers 204 and only ever moves an `sms_log` row forward (error 21610 records the platform STOP).

### Auth (`auth_routes.py`; `/mobile/api/login`, `/register`, `/apple-signin`, `/verify-2fa`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password`, `/logout`, `/me`, `/device-tokens`)
Login (web + mobile variants), mobile register, forgot/reset password, verify-2fa, Apple/Google sign-in, logout, mobile `/me`, APNs token registration.

- Passkeys (9/30/26, `passkeys.py`): sign-in `POST /auth/passkey/options` `{csrf_token}` → `{ok, options}` (WebAuthn request options, no allowCredentials, userVerification required), then `POST /auth/passkey/verify` `{csrf_token, credential, next?}` → `{ok, redirect}` with the session cookie (the session passed its second factor); 401 for a passkey that does not verify or no login saved, 403 for an internal login, a reset-pending one, or no CSRF token; 429 past the per-address throttle. Account → Security: `GET /api/passkeys` → `{passkeys: [{id, name, created, last_used, backed_up}]}`; `POST /api/passkeys/options` `{password}` → `{options}` (403 for a wrong password or a view-as session); `POST /api/passkeys` `{credential}` → `{passkey: {id, name}}`; `POST /api/passkeys/<id>/remove`. All CSRF-checked.
- Passkeys in the app (iOS parity #57, 10/7/26): bearer twins on the same bodies (`auth_routes._do_passkey_login_options`, `_passkey_signin_user`, `_do_passkeys_list`, `_do_passkey_register_options`, `_do_passkey_register`, `_do_passkey_remove`), against `passkeys.app_host()` — the site's own host from `BASE_URL`, whatever host the request reached, the relying party in the app's `webcredentials:dashboard.cavnar.ai` entitlement, so one passkey works in the app and on the site. Sign-in: `POST /mobile/api/passkey/options` → `{ok, options}`; `POST /mobile/api/passkey/verify` `{credential, device_id}` → `{ok, token, user, requires_2fa: false, via: "passkey"}` — the session `/mobile/api/login` would hand back, `device_type` ios, its second factor passed; 403 for a turned-off login, a reset-pending one (`password_reset_required`) or an internal login; 401 for one that doesn't verify; 429 past the per-address throttle. Signed in: `GET /mobile/api/passkeys`, `POST /mobile/api/passkeys/options` `{password}` (403 wrong password or view-as), `POST /mobile/api/passkeys` `{credential}`, `POST /mobile/api/passkeys/<id>/remove`.
- Sign in with Apple on the web (shown only when `APPLE_WEB_SERVICES_ID` is set): `POST /auth/apple/nonce` → `{nonce (SHA-256 of the raw value in the a_sso_nonce cookie), client_id, redirect_uri, state}`, fetched on page load; `POST /auth/apple/web` `{csrf_token, id_token, next?}` → `{ok, redirect}` or 401/403 `{error}`. `/auth/apple/callback` (the registered return URL) only redirects to `/login`.

- 2FA sign-in: `POST /mobile/api/login` answers `{requires_2fa: true, pending_token, masked_email, channel: "sms"|"email"}` when a code was sent; the web renders `two_fa.html` with the same `channel` ("We texted…" / "We emailed…"). An internal login on an authenticator app (R10) gets `channel: "app"` (nothing is sent; `two_fa.html` asks for the app's code and offers no Resend). `POST /verify-2fa` (form) / `POST /mobile/api/verify-2fa` `{pending_token, code}` take the 6-digit code (the app's, for an app login — `auth.check_sign_in_code`; wrong app codes are also throttled per login, 429 on mobile) or a backup code — the backup code in any case, with or without the dash or spaces (`models.normalize_backup_code`; single use).
- Since 9/29/26 `POST /mobile/api/login` also returns `code_sent`, `fell_back` (the text failed or the number sent STOP, so the code went by email) and `delivery_error` (`ok` stays true when `code_sent` is false); the web's `two_fa.html` says which way the code went. An admin or support login is asked for ITS OWN second factor on every path; a Google (`?error=use_password_for_2fa`) or Apple (403) sign-in by an enrolled internal login is refused unless the device is remembered.
- `POST /resend-2fa` and `POST /mobile/api/resend-2fa` `{pending_token}` — one body (`auth_routes.resend_two_fa`): a fresh code for this sign-in to the place the first went → `{ok, channel, masked, fell_back}`; 401 `{ok:false, error}` for a token login() did not issue; 429 when the per-address `2fa-resend:<ip>` throttle trips (every request counts); **502** `{ok: false, channel, error}` when the code did not go (the sender raised or reported failure — it used to answer ok); **400** `{ok: false, channel: "app", error}` for an authenticator-app login — nothing is ever sent to one, and there is no email fallback (a backup code is the way in without the phone). The mobile route is on `auth._UNGATED_PREFIXES`.
- Turning a restaurant's 2FA on: `POST /api/send-2fa-test` `{method: email|sms}` (the account holder's; CSRF-checked) sends a setup code to the login turning it on → `{ok, masked, method}`; **502** `{ok:false, error}` when the send failed (the sender reported failure — it used to answer "Code sent"), 500 when it raised; the setup challenge is removed either way so a retry is not refused. `POST /api/verify-2fa-setup` `{code}` turns it on.

### Staff app (`/staff/*`; `staff_routes.py` and the modules it imports — employee-app fix round, 10/2/26)
Two clients, one API (parity audit #100, owner 10/7/26 — staff without an iPhone, Android included): the iPhone app (Bearer token) and the **browser portal** (the PIN cookie session). `GET /staff/` and `/staff/signup` are the landing page — "Use it in your browser" (the restaurant code, or `/staff/home` when this browser is signed in) and the app (the App Store button when `IOS_APP_STORE_URL` is set, else the TestFlight line); `GET /staff/r/<code>` (throttled; the join code or the portal token) is the browser PIN pad — the `/staff/api/roster` roster, the one-shot nonce, `POST /staff/r/<code>/login`; `GET /staff/home` (staff session; else a 302 to `/staff/`) is the portal, `staff_portal.html`: Today, Schedule, Requests, Tasks, Inbox and Me, every read and write a `fetch()` to the `/staff/api/*` routes below — the page holds no rule of its own (`tests/test_staff_web_portal.py` checks every path it names is a staff route); `?ready=1` after a web sign-up is the "You're all set" page with the restaurant code. `GET /staff/logout` asks; `POST /staff/logout` (form, `csrf_token`) ends the session and redirects to the restaurant's PIN pad. `GET /staff/signup` is the opt-in URL registered with Twilio for the staff verification campaign (9/28/26; its screenshot is `/static/sms/staff-signup-optin.png`). `POST /staff/r/<code>/login` and `/staff/api/*` are the staff tier's only API; there are no mobile twins.

**Conventions.** Authenticated routes take the staff session as the `staff_session` cookie or `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (`auth.staff_login_required`) and answer **401 `{ok:false, error, session_expired: true}`** without one; a console login gets 403. A state-changing request the **cookie** authenticates (no Bearer header) must echo the `csrf_js` cookie as `X-CSRF` (or a `csrf_token` form field) — **403 "Request blocked (CSRF)"** otherwise (`staff_routes.staff_csrf_check`, on `staff_bp` and `staff_knowledge_bp`); the pre-session routes (PIN sign-in, sign-up, forgot PIN) are exempt. The restaurant and the employee always come from the session — no route takes an employee id. Every `/staff/api/` response is `Cache-Control: no-store` (`http_layer._NO_STORE_PREFIXES`). A JSON body that is not an object is a 400 (`security.json_object_guard` on `staff_bp`, `staff_knowledge_bp`). Routes marked *throttled* share the per-address portal budget (`auth.portal_attempts`: 30 failures or 300 requests in 5 minutes, then 429). Errors are `{ok:false, error:"<a sentence to show>"}`.

**Sign-in, account and identity** (`staff_routes`, `staff_account_routes`; B1):
- `POST /staff/r/<code>/login` `{membership_id, pin, nonce, device_id?}` (throttled): the attempt is counted before the code lookup and before the PIN's KDF runs (`auth._reserve_pin_attempt`), so parallel guesses cannot outrun the lockout; a locked login reads "Too many tries. Ask a manager to unlock, or wait N min." `device_id` returns the bearer `token`.
- Sign-up: `POST /staff/api/signup/start` (a US mobile only — `STAFF_OTP_COUNTRY_CODES`; "We can't send codes right now…" past the hourly ceiling `STAFF_OTP_HOURLY_CAP`; `dev_code` only with `STAFF_SIGNUP_DEV_CODE=1`, no Twilio and no deployment), `/signup/verify`, `GET /signup/where/<code>`, `GET /signup/claimable/<code>` (throttled; the signup token in the `X-Signup-Token` header — `?signup_token=` still read for older app builds), `POST /signup/claim` (token in the body or the header; one transaction; **409 `{has_account: true, employee_name}`** when this phone already has a login here — the app sends them to Forgot PIN). The sign-up routes resolve the 6-character join code only; sign-in, the roster and forgot PIN take the join code or the long portal token.
- `POST /staff/api/logout` (no session needed) `{apns_token?}` → `{ok, signed_out: true, was_signed_in}` — deletes the bearer's (or cookie's) session and unregisters this phone's staff push (every phone of theirs here without `apns_token`); always 200.
- Forgot PIN by text (throttled): `POST /staff/api/pin/forgot/start` `{join_code|code, phone}` → the same `{ok, message}` whether or not the number has a login (no text when it has none, nothing when it matches more than one login); `POST …/forgot/verify` `{phone, code}` → `{ok, reset_token (15 minutes, single use), employee_name, restaurant}`, one message for every failure; `POST …/forgot/set` `{reset_token, pin, device_id?}` → `{ok, signed_in: true, redirect, employee_name, membership_id, restaurant, portal_token, token?}` — the PIN policy applies, the lockout is lifted, this login's other staff sessions here end, and it signs in.
- `POST /staff/api/pin` (Change PIN): 401 `{locked: false}` for a wrong current PIN; after 3 misses 401 `{locked: true, signed_out: true}` and the session ends (its own counter, `pin_change_attempts`; sign-in is untouched); 400 with the PIN policy's sentence.
- `POST /staff/api/account/delete` `{confirm: true}` (the JSON boolean only) → `{ok, deleted: true, signed_out: true}`; 400 without the confirm (`auth.delete_own_staff_account`, *DATABASE_SCHEMA* → Staff portal).
- `GET /staff/api/me` → `employee` gains `phone_masked`, `has_phone`, `email`, `notifications: {push, texts, texts_consent, email}` (`texts` = a text would really go: consented, staff SMS configured, no STOP), `restaurant_id`, `membership_id`, `locations: [{restaurant_id, restaurant, membership_id, employee_name, portal_token, join_code, current}]`; each part degrades to empty on its own error. `POST /staff/api/me/email` `{email}` (`""` clears) writes the roster contact (`staff_contacts.email`) — the address the owner sees and notices fall back to.
- `POST /staff/api/switch` `{restaurant_id}` → `{ok, requires_pin: true, restaurant_id, restaurant, membership_id, employee_name, portal_token, join_code, login_nonce}` — what that location's PIN pad needs; it never mints a session (each location's PIN is its own credential). 404 for a location they have no login at.

**Push and notices** (`staff_device_routes`, B2): `POST /staff/api/device-tokens` `{apns_token|token, environment: sandbox|production}` files the phone under the session's login at the staff restaurant with `device_tokens.tier = 'staff'` (call after every sign-in) → `{ok, push_registered, reminders, reminders_server_side: true, quiet_hours: {start: "22:00", end: "08:00"}, alert_types, tabs}`; `DELETE /staff/api/device-tokens[/<apns_token>]` `{apns_token?}` → `{ok, removed}`; `GET /staff/api/notifications` the same object; `POST /staff/api/notifications` `{reminders: bool}` mutes or restores `staff_reminder` on their phones (400 without it). `/mobile/api/device-tokens` still refuses a PIN session. The staff app does not schedule local reminders: the `staff_reminders` job sends them.
- **The payload** (`cavnar` beside `aps`): `alert_type` — one of `push.STAFF_ALERT_TYPES`: `staff_schedule` (tab `today`), `staff_request` (`requests`), `staff_notice` (`inbox`), `staff_reminder` (`today`; a task reminder `tasks`), `staff_announcement` (`inbox`), `staff_urgent` (`inbox`, P1 — time-sensitive, never quiet, not muteable) and `staff_message` (`inbox`), all P2 but the urgent one, every one uncollapsible; `module: "staff"`; `kind` (`schedule`, `request`, `notice`, `reminder`, `announcement`, `urgent`, `message`); `tab` (`push.STAFF_TABS`: today, tasks, requests, me, inbox — `messages` is read as `inbox`); `nav` `staff/<tab>[/<id>]` from the first of `request_id`, `announcement_id`, `thread_id`, `assignment_id`; optional `event`, `request_id`, `request_kind`, `announcement_id`, `thread_id`, `assignment_id`, `line_id`, `schedule_id`, `shift_date`, `shift_start`, `urgent`, and `quiet: true` for a push sent passive between 10pm and 8am restaurant time. A staff device never gets a `badge`.
- `GET/POST /staff/api/preferences` — preferred dayparts, desired hours and `schedule_texts` (their own "text me", Friction #17; a POST of `{schedule_texts}` alone sets only that). The GET adds `sms_available` (false while `TWILIO_STAFF_MESSAGING_SERVICE_SID` is unset — hide the switch), `schedule_texts_consent` (`people.STAFF_SMS_CONSENT_TEXT`, shown verbatim), `schedule_texts_consent_version` (2) and `schedule_texts_scope` (`["schedule","request"]`, `["schedule"]` for a consent given on the old wording, or `[]`); `POST {schedule_texts, consent_version: 2}` records the v2 scope (without `consent_version`, schedule texts only).

**Shifts and requests** (`staff_routes`, `shift_requests`, `time_off`; B3, B8):
- `GET /staff/api/shifts` → `{published, week_start, week_of, week_hours, today (+ legs[] on a double), week: [{date, weekday, is_today, off, posted, shift, shifts[]}], upcoming[]}`; "today" is the restaurant's service date (`staff_schedule.restaurant_today`, so a closer at 12:30am is on last night's shift); every leg carries `start`, `end`, `shift_start`, `shift_end`, `hours`, `notes`, `break`, `station?` (a kitchen cook), `section?` (a floor section, only when one is assigned and still named), `request: {id, kind: drop|swap|post, status: pending|approved|open}|null` and `actions: ["drop","swap"]` (`[]` once a request is live or the shift has started). `posted: false` means "Not posted yet", not "Off". Reading it counts the week as seen (`models.mark_schedule_shares_seen_in_app`).
- `GET /staff/api/shift-requests` → four projections: `requests` (their own, with `reason` and `decision_note`, no `decided_by`; statuses add `cancelled` — the shift changed hands first — and `expired`), `asks` (swaps asked of them, no `reason`), `offers` (`[{id, request_id, status: "offered", date, shift_start, shift_end, role, employee_name|null, note, for_coverage, created_at, pickup_overtime_note}]`) and `open` (no `reason`, `decided_by` or `replacement_name`; their own shift and other roles' left out; `can_take` / `why_not` for the first 10, `posted_by_manager`, `pickup_overtime_note` — "This 7h pickup takes you past 40 hours that week (to 41h).", hours only, null when they cannot take it).
- `POST /staff/api/shift-requests` (drop or swap; the role is checked both ways before the colleague is asked) and `POST …/<id>/respond` refuse a shift that has already started; one live request per shift (a partial unique index backs it). `POST …/<id>/withdraw` covers pending, approved and open drops and swaps (not a manager's `post`). `POST /staff/api/open-shifts/<id>/claim` (legality is `schedule_engine.replacement_is_legal`, the owner's pickers' check — a staffing gap the shift already had, such as nobody until close or a floor short, is the week's and is not charged to whoever takes it; an offer-only shift is "not open"; claiming a manager's extra shift appends it to the published week). New: `POST /staff/api/offers/<offer_id>/respond` `{accept: bool}` → `{ok, offer: {id, status: accepted|declined}, request}` — a yes writes the week (and answers a coverage issue), a no tells the managers.
- `GET /staff/api/colleagues?date=` adds `coworkers: [{name, role, station, shift_start, shift_end}]` (who's on with them that day), `date` and `posted`; with `shift_date` + `shift_start` the swap picker lists only people with an active staff login and only that week's shifts whose trade passes the role and legality check both ways (`checked: false` past a 2.5-second budget).
- `GET /staff/api/time-off` rows add `can_cancel` and `still_scheduled: [{date, day, shift_start, shift_end, role}]` and drop `decided_by`; `POST /staff/api/time-off/<id>/cancel` calls off APPROVED time off that has not started (status `withdrawn`, the deciders told).
- `GET /staff/api/availability` → `{days, week: [{day, status: any|off|window, earliest, latest, from, until}] (7, Monday first), unavailable_days, notes, updated_at, time_off_hint}`; `POST` `{updated_at, week|unavailable_days, notes?}` — `updated_at` is required and must be the value the screen loaded (`staff_settings.save_own_availability`: **409 `{stale: true, availability}`** when the record moved on, so an app whose load failed can never save an empty week; the `/s/` link saves through the same function). 200 adds `conflicts: [{date, day, shift_start, shift_end, role, reason}]`, `conflicts_text`, `hint` (a note that reads like dates away points to time off) and `managers_told`; 400 for a bad entry or all seven days off. The legacy `{unavailable_days}` body changes only which days are off.
- `POST /staff/api/running-late` `{date?, shift_start?, eta_minutes (1–120), note?}` (B5): their own shift on today's published week only; writes attendance `late` from `self_report` (the weakest source), tells the deciders once (`employee_message`), holds the "hasn't clocked in" issue until start + ETA + 15 minutes, or annotates it when already open; 10 an hour per login (429). `GET` → today's `reports`, `eta_choices` [10, 20, 30, 45], `eta_max`.

**Today, tasks and the brief:**
- `GET /staff/api/preshift` (`staff_brief.personal`; B7) → `{day, weekday, working: true|false|null, published, you: [{kind: you|station, text}], items, brief_text, brief_approved, focus: {item, line}|null, language, translated, message?}`. A day off is `working: false` with nothing to read; `null` means no published week (the card shows). `items` kinds: you, station, volume, rush, watch, eighty_sixed, stock, event, promotion, weather. `brief_text` is only text a manager approved, translated when the reader set a language and the translation passed.
- `GET /staff/api/tasks` (B4): send `X-Staff-Tasks-Version: 2` and the legacy flat `tasks` list is left out; adds `last_night_note: {note, signed_by, signed_at, shift_kind, task_date, date_label}|null` (a manager with an opening sheet only). `POST /staff/api/tasks/complete` and `POST /staff/api/tasks/photo` answer `{ok, late, flagged, alert, sheet, task_date}` — `sheet` is the ticked sheet as it now stands (replace it by `id`; no second GET); `alert: {critical: true, title: "Tell your manager now", message, manager_alerted}` for a critical line read out of range (it opens a high `task_flag` issue). A tick outside the business day ±1 is refused ("That sheet is from another day…"). The photo is multipart (`file` or `photo`) or JSON base64; it is stored only once the sheet is the caller's, re-encoded to 1280 px JPEG, at most 60 a login an hour. `GET /staff/api/tasks/photo/<token>` is `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.

**Inbox and messages** (`staff_comms_routes`; B5, S9): `GET /staff/api/inbox` → `{announcements: [{id, title, body, priority, created_at, created_by_name, expires_on, acked_at, language, translated, original_title, original_body}], unread, unread_messages}` (unread first; withdrawn and expired left out; a translation read from the cache only — the inbox never calls a model); `POST /staff/api/announcements/<id>/ack` → `{ok, id, acked_at}` (idempotent; 404 when not theirs); `GET /staff/api/messages[?mark_read=0]` → `{thread_id, unread, messages: [{id, from: staff|manager, sender_name, body, shift_date, request_id, request_kind, created_at, read_at}]}` (one thread per login with the managers on duty; opening it marks the managers' replies read; on a staff message `read_at` is "Seen"); `POST /staff/api/messages` `{body (≤1000), shift_date?, request_id?, request_kind?: shift|time_off}` — the request must be theirs; the deciders are pushed once per burst (10 minutes) as `employee_message`; 30 an hour per login.

**Me** (`staff_me_routes`, `staff_knowledge_routes`; B6, B7) — the caller's own figures only, never anyone else's and never a score:
- `GET /staff/api/earnings?days=` (1–60, default 14) → their hours and tips per shift from the POS punches by their own POS id (`available: false` with `reason: pos_not_connected|not_found|ambiguous` rather than zeros; `as_of`, `lag_note`, `tips_note`; no pay).
- `GET /staff/api/stats` → the payroll week's scheduled and worked hours, an overtime heads-up in hours against 40, their own attendance for 30 days (`tracked: false` when nobody watched), their certifications.
- `GET /staff/api/recognition` → confirmed positive guest mentions of them in the last 365 days, each with a safe excerpt or `null` (no reviewer, platform or colleague).
- `GET /staff/api/pulse` → `{due, recent}`; `POST {date, rating 1–5, note? ≤300}` — once per own published shift, from its start to three business days back (409 otherwise).
- `GET /staff/api/calendar-link` → `{url, webcal_url, created_at, last_fetched_at, note}` (the same URL each call); `POST …/reset` a new URL (the old one 410); `POST …/revoke` → `{revoked}`. The feed `GET /staff/cal/<token>.ics` (no session; throttled) serves their live published shifts 14 days back to 60 ahead; 404 unknown, 410 revoked or the holder deactivated.
- `GET/POST /staff/api/language` `{language}` (en, es, pt, fr, zh, vi, ko, tl, ar, ru, pl, ht). `GET /staff/api/docs` → `{house_rules, docs (their job roles' or everyone's), certifications (their own: cert, expires_on, days_left, status), can_ask}`. `POST /staff/api/ask` `{question ≤300}` → `{answered, answer, reason, suggest_message, sources: [{id, source, kind, line}]}` — an answer only from the house rules and docs, citing stored lines; pay, other people and discipline are refused before any model call; 4 a minute and 30 a day (429 `{suggest_message: true}`).

### The staff app, owner side (employee-app fix round, 10/2/26)
- **Team inbox** (`staff_comms_routes`, web `/api/…` + mobile `/mobile/api/…`, the Labor module and `SCHEDULE_DRAFT` or an admin; every answer `no-store`): `GET labor/inbox` → `{unread, threads: [{thread_id, membership_id, employee_name, last_body, last_from, last_shift_date, last_at, unread}], late_today: [...]}`; `GET labor/inbox/threads/<id>[?mark_read=0]` (reading marks the staff messages read for the whole team; 404 for another restaurant's); `POST labor/inbox/threads/<id>/reply` `{body}` → `{ok, message, delivered_via}`; `GET labor/inbox/announcements` → `{announcements, roles, audiences: [all, role, shift_date], priorities: [normal, urgent]}` (each with `read_line` "9 of 14 read", `unread_names`, `read_by`, `delivered` counts); `POST labor/inbox/announcements` `{title ≤120, body ≤2000, priority, audience, audience_value, expires_on?}` → `{ok, announcement, delivery}` (the audience fixed at send, one recipient row per active staff login; each recipient's copy translated into their language at delivery; 20 an hour per restaurant); `GET labor/inbox/announcements/<id>`; `POST …/<id>/withdraw`. A manager is pushed as **`employee_message`** (P2, Labor, uncollapsible, in `notify.BRIEFING_ALWAYS` and `models.NON_ALERT_TYPES` so no briefing level, budget or alert cap holds it; bell label "A message from your team"; nav `labor/inbox[/<thread_id>]`; with a `thread_id` its category is `CAVNAR_MESSAGE`, a typed lock-screen Reply that posts `labor/inbox/threads/<id>/reply {body}`). The iOS Team inbox (parity 10/7/26 #10) is these routes: opening a thread is the same marking read, so staff see "Seen".
- **The lineup brief** (`staff_knowledge_routes`, both prefixes): `GET staff-brief[?day=]` (any console login) → `{can_edit (SCHEDULE_PUBLISH), brief: {day, weekday, items, draft_text, draft_status: none|drafted|refused|held|failed|no_items, draft_reason, draft_items_changed, model_used_today, approved_text, approved_at, approved_by, edited, focus, suggestions?}}`; `POST staff-brief/draft` (at most one model call a day; 429 past 3 presses a minute), `POST staff-brief/approve {day?, text?}` (no text approves the draft; a manager's own text ≤600 characters is held to the staff rule), `POST staff-brief/withdraw`, `POST staff-brief/focus {item ≤80, line ≤160}` — each SCHEDULE_PUBLISH, today or tomorrow only. A draft the pre-shift nudge wrote that passed is announced as **`lineup_brief_waiting`** (P2, Labor, `notify.BRIEFING_ALWAYS`, `models.NON_ALERT_TYPES`, bell label "Tonight's lineup brief is waiting", nav `labor/lineup`, payload `day`, category `CAVNAR_LINEUP`: Approve posts `staff-brief/approve {day, text: null}`) to the SCHEDULE_PUBLISH logins (`staff_brief.announce_waiting`, `strategy_jobs._reach` deciders); a manager's own `staff-brief/draft` is not announced (parity 10/7/26 #26).
- **House rules, staff docs and certifications** (both prefixes): `GET/POST house-rules` and `GET/POST staff-docs`, `POST staff-docs/<id>/remove` (writes need TEAM_INVITE; kinds menu_spec, allergens, sop, training, other, each with `roles`, body ≤20,000); `GET/POST staff-certs`, `POST staff-certs/<id>/remove` (writes need SCHEDULE_PUBLISH; upsert on person and certificate, `expires_on`, `remind_days: 30`). An expired certificate no longer counts toward a role's requirement in the schedule rules.

### Public links (fix round, 9/29/26)
- **Pay links** `GET /pay/<token>/<period>` (on `webhook_bp`): the token is `<rid>.v<n>.<sig>` — signed with the kept secret `pay_links` (the legacy `<rid>.<sig>` still verifies while `SECRET_KEY` is unchanged) — and `period` is `monthly`, `annual`, `card` (a fresh Stripe billing-portal session) or `invoice` (the open invoice's hosted page). In order: a hold → "Your account is on hold"; a covered location → "already covered … billed together with <payer>" (or, when the payer is past due, a redirect to the payer's invoice); past due, or `card` / `invoice` → a redirect to the invoice or the portal (404 "No card on file yet" with no customer); paused → "Paused"; paying → "You're all set"; otherwise a redirect to a new Checkout session. A bad token or period is a 404 page.
- **Join links** `/join/<token>`: `<rid>-v<n>.<sig>` signed with the kept secret `join_links`; the legacy `<rid>-<sig>` still verifies; a bare restaurant id only with `ALLOW_LEGACY_JOIN_LINKS=1`.
- **Unsubscribe** `GET/POST /u/<token>` (CSRF-exempt): a restaurant token (`<rid>.<sig>`) stops that restaurant's marketing email; an ADDRESS token (`a.<the address, base64url>.<sig>`, signed with `email_links`, carried by Cavnar AI's own referral and marketing mail) asks on GET and on POST suppresses that address for marketing only (scope `marketing`).
- **Set-password links** use the reset page, `/reset-password/<token>`: 72 hours for the post-signing welcome, one hour for a reset or the console's Resend welcome.
- **Schedule links** `GET/POST /s/<token>` (employee-app fix round, 10/2/26): the token is stored only as `sha256:<hex>` (`models._share_hash`; plaintext rows migrated at boot), every send mints its own link, and a link ages out 60 days after it was sent (a row with no expiry is backfilled). A link to a week since replaced shows the live copy ("This week's schedule changed on M/D/YY…"); a holder whose logins are all inactive, or whose roster row is inactive, sees it expired. An unknown token is the branded `staff_schedule_invalid.html` (404), and guessing is throttled on the portal's per-address budget (429, the same page). The availability POST saves through `staff_settings.save_own_availability` with the page's `updated_at` and redirects with `?error=throttled|stale|all_days` (stale: "Someone changed this since you opened it — here's the latest").
- **Guest links** `/g/<token>` (a marketing link's tap-and-redirect): an unknown or expired one is `guest_link_expired.html` (404), the guest's own page — it used to be the staff schedule-link error, which told a diner to ask their manager.
- **The staff calendar feed** `GET /staff/cal/<token>.ics` (*Staff app* above): an HMAC token under the kept secret `staff_calendar`, stored as a hash, throttled per address; 410 once revoked or the holder is deactivated.

### Owner routes an operator session cannot use (fix round, 9/29/26)
`/client/upload-data`, `/api/templates*` and `/api/import-tripadvisor` write into the session's own restaurant, which for an admin or support session is the operator's home row: without a numeric `restaurant_id` they answer **400 `{operator_session: true}`**. The console's routes are `/admin/upload-data/<rid>`, `/admin/api/templates/<rid>` and `/admin/import-reviews/<rid>`. The review import now refuses an unknown platform (it used to file it as TripAdvisor). `POST /api/log-activity` answers `{ok, skipped: "view_as"}` for a view-as session and records nothing. The team invite (`POST /api/account/team/invite` and its mobile twin) adds `invite_email_sent` and `invite_email_error`; the email history (`GET /api/email-history`, `/mobile/api/account/email-history`) returns `sent_at` in UTC plus `sent_at_local` (the restaurant's wall time) — render `sent_at_local`.

### The phone's Live Activities and widget pushes (`live_activities.py`, `push.py`; iOS parity audit 10/7/26 #31, #38, #61, #94)
- `GET /api/intraday/tonight` and `GET /mobile/api/intraday/tonight` (`strategy_routes._ROUTES`, one body, Labor-gated, LABOR_VIEW — 403 otherwise) → `{ok, in_service, business_date, opens_at, closes_at, closes_at_unix, attributes, content_state}` — tonight's service as the "Tonight's service" Live Activity draws it, built by the same `live_activities.service_content` every push uses: `attributes` is `ServiceAttributes` (`restaurantId`, `restaurantName`, `businessDate`, `dayLabel` "Fri 10/9/26") and `content_state` its ContentState (`status` open | closed, `pulseLine` "▲ 8% vs a typical Fri" + `pulseUp`, or `pulseNote` saying why there is none — never 0%; `missing` (the first four names on the published schedule who haven't clocked in, from the coverage check's last read — the route never calls the POS), `missingCount`, `coverageNote`, `closesAt`, `updatedAt`; the Dates are seconds since 2001, `push.apple_date`). No dollar figure.
- `POST /mobile/api/live-activity-tokens` `{activity_type, kind, token, environment, activity_key}` → `{ok}` — files a phone's Live Activity push token under the calling session (`push.register_live_activity_token`): `kind` `start` (a push-to-start token, iOS 17.2+; one per device per `activity_type` — `pending_send`, `schedule_build`, `service` — a login that may not watch it is refused 403: a pending send needs a kind it may undo, a service LABOR_VIEW) or `update` (one running activity's token, `activity_key` the delayed action id, the generation's job id or the service's business date — required). 400 on an unknown type or kind or a token that is not hex. Phone-only (no web twin), like `/device-tokens`. A token is sent to only while that session is live and its login active; signing out stops them all.
- `DELETE /mobile/api/live-activity-tokens` `{activity_type?, kind?}` → `{ok, removed}` — this session's tokens for one type (turning "Tonight's service" off) or all.
- What the server sends (none from a host where `scheduler.scheduling_allowed()` is false): a **silent push** (`push.fire_silent`: `apns-push-type: background`, priority 5, `aps` = `{"content-available": 1}` only, `cavnar: {silent: "dsr" | "waiting", restaurant_id}`; at most one per restaurant, reason and audience per 15 minutes) when a night's report reaches final or provisional (`dsr.deliver.on_terminal`), when an actionable alert goes out (`push.fire_push`), and when a web read of `GET /api/actions` finds that login's count changed (`widget_waiting_counts`); and **Live Activity pushes** (`apns-push-type: liveactivity`, topic `<bundle>.push-type.liveactivity`, start/end at priority 10, update at 5): the queued-send countdown is started on every phone allowed to undo it when `delayed.schedule` queues a countdown kind, and ended ("stopped" / "sent" / "failed") by `delayed.cancel` and `delayed._run_one`; a generation's days drafted reach its activity from `ops.set_async_job_progress` and its end from `ops.finish_async_job`; tonight's service starts at open, updates on change and ends at close from the 20-minute service slot (`strategy_jobs.run_intraday_capture`, with `run_coverage_check`'s names). The `generate-schedule` start answer's `typical` (`{seconds, n, basis}`) is the activity's estimate.

### Owner AI jobs (`ai_async.py`; AI cost audit 10/7/26 #57)
Five routes run a slow model call as a job the client polls when it sends `async` (`1`/`true`/`yes`: a query arg, a multipart form field, or a JSON body field): `POST food-cost/invoices` and `POST food-cost/recipes/scan` (`/api/…` and `/mobile/api/…`, `strategy_routes`), `POST /api/guest-campaign/draft` / `/mobile/api/guest-campaign/draft`, `POST /api/guest-newsletter/draft` / `/mobile/api/guest-newsletter/draft`, and `POST /api/generate-content` / `/mobile/api/marketing/generate-content`.
- The start answers **202** `{ok: true, async: true, status: "pending", job_id, joined, wait_seconds}` once the route's own checks pass (module, permission, rate limit — counted once, on the request — and, for an upload, `invoices.check_upload`: a bad file is the same **400** as before). The same request pressed again while its job runs (the file's sha256, the goal and topic) joins it: `joined: true`, the same `job_id`. An async upload's `Idempotency-Key` is not remembered (the job store joins a repeat; once finished, a repeat runs again — `invoices.scan`'s sha check answers a file already read without a call).
- `GET /api/ai-jobs/<job_id>` / `/mobile/api/ai-jobs/<job_id>` → `{ok: true, status: "pending", job_id, seconds_left?}` while it runs; then **exactly the synchronous route's payload with its status code** (200 with the invoice / draft / message / content; 422 a refused draft; 429 a budget stop; 400 a card that could not be read; 502 / 500 a failure), plus `status: "done"` and `job_id`. **404** for an unknown job, another restaurant's, or another login's (an admin may read any); **503** `{ok: false, status: "error", error: "That took longer than it should and was stopped — nothing was saved. Try again."}` for a job the store failed (a deploy mid-job, its deadline passed — `ai_async.JOB_SECONDS`).
- Without `async` every route answers synchronously, as before — the shape iOS builds in the field rely on. The web (`dashboard.html` `aiJobAwait`) and iOS (`APIClient.resolveAIJob` / `awaitAIJob`) send it and poll at 1.5 s, then 3 s, then every 5 s (iOS parity perf, 10/7/26; `seconds_left` caps the wait).

### Module reads, stale-while-refresh (`insight_refresh.py`; iOS parity audit 10/7/26 #37)
`GET /api/labor-insight`, `/api/review-insight`, `/api/mkt-insight` and their twins `/mobile/api/labor/insight`, `/mobile/api/reviews/insight`, `/mobile/api/marketing/insight` take `async` (query arg, `1`/`true`/`yes`). With it, a read already stored for the current data answers exactly as before; one that is not answers **at once** and is written on the owner AI pool (`ai_async`, kind `insight_<labor|reviews|marketing>`, one per restaurant and read — a second open from any login joins it), through the same orchestrated workflow, budgets and canary:
- the last stored read, whatever its data, with `stale: true`, `as_of` (M/D/YY), `stale_note` ("From a read on 10/6/26 — Cavnar AI is writing a newer one."), `refreshing: true`, `pending: false`, `refresh_job`; or
- with nothing stored, `pending: true`, `status: "pending"`, `refreshing: true`, `refresh_job`, the read's own fields carrying a one-line placeholder (`insight`; on mobile Labor/Marketing also `insight_intro`, `insight_recommendations: []`) so an older build still decodes a sentence.
- The client polls the same route with `async=1&refresh_job=<id>` (1.5 → 3 → 5 s). While the job runs it gets the answer above again without a render; once written, the read itself. A job that wrote nothing (a budget stop, an outage) answers the last read with `stale: true, refreshing: false` and the "couldn't be written" note, else the route's own error and status — never a new job per poll.
- Without `async`, the synchronous answer as before. `dashboard.html` (`insightSwr`) and iOS (`APIClient.sendInsight` + `InsightRefresh.follow`) send it.

### Conditional GETs (`http_layer.conditional_get`; iOS parity audit 10/7/26 #56)
`GET /mobile/api/home`, `/mobile/api/labor`, `/mobile/api/reviews` (first page: no `offset` or `offset=0`), `/mobile/api/dsr` and `/mobile/api/dsr/<day>` answer 200 with a weak `ETag` — a hash of the login, the restaurant and the exact JSON — and `Vary: Authorization`. A request whose `If-None-Match` carries it gets a bodiless **304** (same `ETag`, `Cache-Control: no-store` kept). iOS keeps the tagged body itself (`APIClient` conditional copies, in SecureCache) and reuses it on a 304. JSON over 1 KB is gzipped for a client that sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip` (`compress_response`); a stream (Ask's SSE) is never compressed, buffered or tagged.

An unknown `/mobile/api/…` path answers **404** `{ok: false, unknown_route: true, error}` (the app's catch-all) — as against a route's own 404 for something it looked for and did not find; iOS's review-by-id falls back to paging the inbox only on the former.

### Other groups with web + mobile twins
`/api/tasks*` (the old flat checklist; kept for older apps), `/api/task-sheets*` (Task sheets: `GET` the sheets + job codes + `can_edit`; `POST` create; `POST /<id>` settings (`job_code`, `shift_kind`, `title`, `days_of_week`, `requires_signoff`, `active`); `POST /<id>/lines` add; `POST /lines/<id>` edit or `active:false`; `POST /<id>/order` `{line_ids}`; `POST /<id>/starter` Cavnar AI's draft lines (never saved); `GET /day?date=` the day, read-only; `GET /report?days=` 7–90; `GET /photo/<token>` a proof photo; edits need `_may_manage_team`, a refusal is 403, a bad field 400 with the reason), `/api/team/inbox` + `/api/team/messages*`, `/api/changelog*`, `/api/switch-location` + `/api/group-locations`, `/api/home` + `/api/home/dismiss` (hide / done / not_for_us / snooze `days`, `undo`, `restore_kind`; every answer also written to rec_ledger) + `/api/home/assign` (`{key, title, detail, contact_id}` → an issue keyed to the recommendation; owner only), `/api/rpower/status` and the `/admin/rpower/*` bootstrap.

### Admin (`/admin/*`, the rules on `admin_bp` — `python3 scripts/repo_inventory.py` counts them, 183 on 9/30/26; `/admin/status/*`, `/admin/audits*` and `/admin/<pos>/*` live on their own blueprints)
Client health rollup (owner → brand → location), jobs, billing, AI operations, messaging, the audit trail, support tools, the sales-audit tool (`/admin/audits/*`), changelog authoring, status-page incident management. The console is `templates/admin.html` over `admin_routes.py` → `admin_ops.py` / `admin_events.py`; its `api()` helper answers the guards below itself (asks for the password on `reauth_required` and sends again, opens enrolment on `two_factor_required`, waits `Retry-After` once on 503 busy, says so on 429). The full admin console contract is the next section.

## Admin console API (fix round, 9/29/26)

Every route here is `@admin_required` unless marked, CSRF-checked blueprint-wide, and audited by the request hook after the response (`admin_events`, source `audit`; typed actions add a `record_admin_action` row). Paths take `<rid>` = `<int:restaurant_id>`.

**The answers every admin route may give** (`auth.admin_required`, `auth.recent_auth_required`, `admin_ops.AdminBusy`):

| Status | Body | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 302 → `/login` | — | a page GET with no admin or support session (none, expired, or a client login's) |
| 401 | `{ok:false, error:"Your session expired — please log in again.", session_expired:true}` | the same, for a request that wants JSON (`auth._admin_wants_json`): any write, any GET under `/api/`, `/admin/api/` or `/mobile/api/`, and any request whose `Accept` prefers `application/json` — the console's `api()` sends that on every call (so `/admin/status/*` reads get the 401 too) and then sends the admin to sign in; or an enrolled internal login's session that never passed its second factor (the session is ended; `two_factor_required:true` too) |
| 403 | `{ok:false, error:"Support accounts are read-only."}` | a support login on any write except view-as and its own 2FA enrolment |
| 403 | `{ok:false, error, two_factor_required:true, enrol_url:"/admin/two-factor"}` | `ADMIN_REQUIRE_2FA=1` and this internal login has not enrolled (a page GET redirects there instead) |
| 403 | `{ok:false, reauth_required:true, reauth_url:"/admin/api/reauth", window_minutes:15, error}` | a step-up route and no password typed in 15 minutes: `POST /admin/api/reauth`, then send the action again |
| 429 | `{ok:false, error, rate_limited:true, retry_after}` + `Retry-After` | the per-session ceiling: 240 requests / 60 writes a minute |
| 503 | `{ok:false, busy:true, error, retry_after}` + `Retry-After`, `X-Admin-Busy: 1` | the fleet build (or a heavy analytics read) is already running with a waiter; retry after that many seconds |
| 503 | `{ok:false, error}` | the second-factor gate itself failed (fails closed) |
| 409 | `{ok:false, error, local_backend:true}` or `{ok:false, error}` | an action that would email, text, change Stripe or void a DocuSign envelope, refused on a backend where `scheduler.scheduling_allowed()` is false (the deliberate exceptions are in `docs/ops/SECURITY.md`) |
| 502 | `{ok:false, error}` (+ `reason`) | the provider (Resend, Twilio, Stripe, DocuSign) refused or failed — nothing claims it was sent |

A support login's `/admin/api/` JSON reads are masked (emails, phones, IPs, user agents, Stripe ids; on `admin_bp` and the sales-audit blueprint's `/admin/api/audits*`) and carry `X-Redacted: support`; the legacy HTML client pages (`/admin/client-settings/<rid>`, `/admin/client-data/<rid>`) answer support a 403 page that points at the console. **(step-up)** below marks a route that needs the password typed in the last 15 minutes (the 403 above). Every fleet payload says how fresh it is: `generated_at`, `cached`, `age_seconds`, `errors` / `query_errors` (a client page's own `errors` is its failure list; use `query_errors`), `unavailable`, `windows{tz, labels}` — a failed read is unknown, never zero. `?fresh=1` asks for a build no older than 5 seconds.

**The event catalog** (Event Intelligence phase 2, 10/1/26; Operations → Event calendar)
- `GET /admin/api/event-catalog` → `{ok, series: [{id, slug, name, league, category, followers, needs_result, events: [{id, external_id, week, event_date, kickoff_local, home_away, opponent, broadcast, is_primetime, status, result, overrides, attributes, needs_result}]}], editable, statuses}` — `needs_result` is an if-necessary game whose date has passed with no result and no cancellation (`store.unresolved`: its night stays out of every baseline, unmeasured, until one is entered; the editor shows a "needs a result" chip), and on a series how many of its games are.
- `POST /admin/api/event-catalog/<event_id>` `{changes: {event_date?, kickoff_local?, broadcast?, status?, result?}, clear?: [field…]}` → `{ok, before, after, overrides, followers, job_id, queued, message}`: the correction is kept apart from the season file and laid over every load; a field in `clear` takes the season file's value now. The correction is committed in the request; the following restaurants' re-sync runs on the admin job pool (poll `/admin/api/admin-jobs/<job_id>` → `{synced, failed, left, message}`), one restaurant at a time for at most 120 seconds — any left, or any when the pool is full (`job_id` null, `queued` false), are re-synced by the 5am `event_sync`. 400 for a bad value (named: a 24-hour kickoff, a YYYY-MM-DD date, a known status) or a field that can't be corrected — audited as `refused`; 404 for no such game. Audited as dispatched (`event_catalog.edit`, before/after with `followers` and `resync_job`; `partial` when the re-sync could not be queued); the job audits its own outcome (`event_catalog.resync`: `ok`, or `partial` when a follower failed or was left to 5am).

**Your own login and access** (fix round A)
- `POST /admin/api/reauth` `{password}` → 200 `{ok, valid_minutes:15}`; 403 wrong password; the fifth miss in 15 minutes → 401 `session_expired` and the session is ended. Support cannot use it.
- `GET /admin/two-factor` — the enrolment page (reachable in the `enrol` state). `POST /admin/two-factor/send` `{method: email|sms}` → `{ok, masked, method}`; 400 nowhere to send; 429 within 60 s of the last; 502 send failed. `{method: "app"}` (R10) sends nothing: it makes a pending authenticator secret and answers `{ok, method: "app", qr, secret, issuer, account, expires_minutes}` — `qr` an SVG data URI drawn on the server, `secret` the key in groups of four, `Cache-Control: no-store`; this response is the only place the secret ever appears; 409 without a working `CREDENTIAL_KEY`. `POST /admin/two-factor/verify` `{code, method, next}` → `{ok, backup_codes, method, next}` (stamps the session's second factor); 400 wrong, expired, or (app) a QR code that is no longer the current one; 429 (app) after 5 wrong codes in 15 minutes for this login. A login that already has two-factor may call both to **switch** method (to the app, the app to a new phone, or back to email/SMS): they then need the step-up (403 `{reauth_required}`), `send` answers 409 for the method already in use, and `verify` answers `{ok, method, switched: true, next}` — the backup codes are kept. `POST /admin/two-factor/disable` (step-up) → `{ok}`; 409 while `ADMIN_REQUIRE_2FA=1`. `POST /admin/two-factor/backup-codes` (step-up) → `{ok, backup_codes}`.
- `POST /admin/api/me/username` (step-up) `{username}` → `{ok, username}`; 400; 409 taken.
- `GET /admin/api/users/<id>/security` → `{ok, user{id, username, role, is_active, internal, must_reset_password}, two_factor{scope: login|restaurant, enabled, method, backup_codes_left}, lockout{…}, sessions[{session_id, device_type, is_view_as, created_at, last_active, expires_at, token_hint, ip_address?, user_agent?}], trusted_devices (a count)}` — support gets no IPs or user agents. `GET /admin/api/lockouts` → `{ok, lockouts[{username, locked, seconds_left, failures_15m, failures_24h, addresses[{ip, locked, seconds_left, failures_15m}], user_id, restaurant_id}]}` — `user_id` / `restaurant_id` name the login the lock is on (null for a name that is no login), so Clear can be offered on every locked login; support gets no IPs.
- Step-up writes, each audited: `POST /admin/api/users/<id>/reset-2fa` → `{ok, scope}` (scope `restaurant` turns 2FA off for everyone at that restaurant); `POST /admin/api/users/<id>/clear-lockout` → `{ok, cleared}`; `POST /admin/api/users/<id>/sessions/<session_id>/revoke` (16-hex handle) → `{ok}`; `POST /admin/api/users/<id>/revoke-sessions` → `{ok, sessions_ended, devices_forgotten}` (400 on yourself).
- `GET /admin/api/support-logins` → `{ok, logins}`; `POST` (step-up) `{username, email}` → `{ok, user_id, reset_link_sent, note}` (a random password; the reset link is emailed only where sending is allowed); 409 taken.
- `POST /admin/deactivate-client/<user_id>` (step-up) → `{ok, sessions_ended, devices_forgotten}`; 404 missing or an admin row. `POST /admin/reactivate-client/<user_id>` → `{ok, emailed, email_note}` (emails only an account holder whose billing allows access, only where sending is allowed).
- `POST /admin/api/set-user-role` (step-up) `{user_id, role: client|owner|manager|member}` → `{ok, role, previous_role}`; every refusal is 400 (not found, admin/support/staff rows, the last owner).
- **View-as**: `GET /admin/view-as/<rid>` asks; `POST` opens a 2-hour session on the restaurant's OWNER login (404 when there is none) and redirects to `/`, with a path-scoped return cookie; support's is read-only. `GET /admin/stop-viewing` asks; `POST` ends only a view-as session and restores the admin's own (not `admin_required` — it runs on the client's session; CSRF-checked). Writes made through a view-as that its read-only flag refuses answer 403 `{read_only:true}`.

**Support, lifecycle and the audit trail** (fix round B2)
- Reset and welcome (all **step-up**): `POST /admin/send-reset-link/<user_id>`, `/admin/reset-password/<user_id>`, `/admin/reset-password-by-restaurant/<rid>` all send a one-hour reset LINK (a `password` in the body is ignored: `password_ignored`) → `{ok, email, sent_to, username, message}`; 403 an admin target or a support caller; 409 a staff PIN identity, an inactive login or a local backend; 502 the send failed. `POST /admin/resend-welcome/<rid>` → `{ok, email, sent_to, username, signed_in_before, message}` — THE welcome (`emails.send_welcome_with_set_password_link`, the one the post-signing outbox and checkout provisioning send) to the principal login's own address: its username and a one-use set-password link good for 3 days (`models.SET_PASSWORD_LINK_HOURS`, 72); the current password keeps working. 200 `{already_sent:true}` inside a 5-minute cooldown; 404 no restaurant or no owner login; 409 churned / canceled, deletion requested, no address, an inactive login, a local backend, or `{suppressed:true, email}` (the address is on the suppression list — lift it first); 502 any other failure (the cooldown is given back).
- Takeover: `POST /admin/freeze/<rid>` (**step-up**) `{reason?}` → `{ok, frozen}` (the number of logins frozen: every session and trusted device revoked, a password reset required before the next sign-in; audited `account_frozen`).
- Test sends: `POST /admin/test-digest/<rid>`, `/admin/test-urgent/<rid>` `{to?: "me"}` → `{ok, sent_to, to, message}`; 409 local backend; 502.
- Demo (**step-up**): `POST /admin/api/client/<rid>/demo` `{is_demo, confirm_name}` → `{ok, restaurant_id, is_demo}`; 400 `confirm_required` (turning it on needs the typed name); 409 refused (a Stripe customer, a signed contract, active / past_due / paused billing, or an admin login living on it). `POST /admin/api/client/<rid>/delete-demo` (**step-up**) → `{ok, rows, tables}`. `POST /admin/seed-reviews/<rid>` — demo accounts only (400 otherwise) → `{ok, seeded, job_id, message}` (drafting runs on the admin pool).
- Admin jobs, all on the one admin job pool: `POST /admin/redraft-all/<rid>` (the job's result is `{ok, redrafted, kept_owner_edits, skipped, failed, remaining, message, error?}` — an owner edit is never overwritten, even one saved while its reply was being written: `draft_response(..., unedited_only=True)` → `models.update_draft` checks `draft_edited` again), `/admin/refresh-ig-token/<rid>` (404 no Instagram token; the job's result is `{ok, expires, error, refreshed{instagram, facebook?}, expires_on, message}` — `ok` is false when the Facebook page token did not move), `/admin/seed-review-account` (`{rotate_password: true, confirm: "ROTATE"}` to rotate; 400 `confirm_required`; rotating is **step-up**, keeping the password is not) → `{ok, job_id, joined}`; 429 the pool is full. Poll `GET /admin/api/admin-jobs/<id>` → 200 `{status:"pending"}`, 200 the job's result, **502** a failed job's result with `error`, 404 `{status:"missing"}`; a read-once value (`password_once`) is removed after the first admin read.
- Offboarding (#34): `GET /admin/api/deletion-requests` (soonest due first); `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/offboarding` → `{request{requested_at, due_on, days_left, overdue}, connected, steps[{step, label, hint, status: pending|done|skipped|not_needed, actor, note}], outstanding, ready_to_delete}`; `POST …/offboarding/<step>` `{status: done|skipped|pending, note}` → the checklist (`revoked` beside it when a step acted); 400 an unknown step or status, or a skip with no note; 404. Marking an acting step done CARRIES IT OUT and is **step-up** (`offboarding.ACTING_STEPS`): `integrations` clears every stored credential and turns outbound webhooks off (500 when that failed); `stripe` cancels every live subscription on the customer (`billing_jobs.cancel_subscription`) — 409 `{covered:true, billed_by}` for a location another's subscription covers (change the plan on the payer, then skip), 409 `{local_backend:true}` on a server that is not production, 502 when Stripe refused; `docusign` voids the envelope waiting for a signature (`docusign_helper.void_envelope`; `contract_status` becomes `voided`) — 409 `{local_backend:true}`, 409 when the envelope is already signed (skip it with that note), 502 when DocuSign refused. Every mark is audited `offboarding.<step>`. `POST …/deletion/withdraw` `{note}` → `{ok, withdrawn, message}`; 409 none open, or it changed since the page loaded. `POST /admin/api/client/<rid>/delete` (**step-up**) `{confirm_name}` → `{ok, restaurant_id, rows, tables}`; 400 name mismatch; 409 an admin or support login calls it home (`models.delete_restaurant` also refuses one, `InternalLoginHome`) or `{outstanding}` steps remain; 500 rolled back.
- `POST /admin/api/brand/add-location` (**step-up**) `{from_restaurant_id, restaurant_name, location_name?, google_place_id?, yelp_business_id?, owner_phone?, timezone? (default: the brand's), module_*? (default: the brand's), audit_id?}` — a location under the brand's owner, group and organization, no new login; the owner becomes `owner` with memberships at both; billing inherited (covered by the paying location); 400 / 409 (a group name another owner uses).
- Notes: `GET` / `POST /admin/api/client/<rid>/notes` `{body}` — append-only, authored, dated (support may read, not write); `legacy_internal_notes` returned read-only. Bug reports: `GET /admin/api/bug-reports?status=open|closed&restaurant_id=&before_id=&limit=`, `POST /admin/api/bug-reports/<id>/status` `{status, note?}`.
- Audit: `GET /admin/api/audit?restaurant_id=&actor=&action=<prefix>&result=&before_id=&limit=&all=1` → `{ok, events[{id, created_at, source, kind: action|request, action, restaurant_id, restaurant, actor, target, result, ip, summary, before, after, request{status, method, path, body, role}, request_id}], next_before_id}`; `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/audit` (same shape; still answers after the client is deleted); `GET /admin/api/audit/refused` → `{ok, attempts, next_before_id}`. Rows written by `admin_events.record` (not `record_admin_action`) have no actor/before/after columns — since the integration wave only events no console login makes (webhooks, provisioning, break-glass and boot events, an owner's close-account request) and the vendor-cost entry; a phone in a stored request body keeps its last four digits.
- Sales audits: `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/audits`; link an audit to a client `POST /admin/api/audits/<id>/link` (audited); share links are hashed and expire after 90 days. `GET /admin/audits/new` only asks (a page with a POST form, which support may see); `POST /admin/audits/new` creates the audit and redirects to it.

**Legacy client pages, data and tools** (fix round B1)
- `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/settings` → `{ok, restaurant_id, name, version, settings, fields, labels, step_up_fields, choices{timezone, billing_status, pos_system, inventory_frequency, digest_day}, save_url}` — the legacy settings page's data as JSON (every field the save accepts, as stored; `weekly_revenue_target` is not stored, so it is absent); 404. Save through the route below with `expected_version` = `version` and `base` from `settings`.
- `POST /admin/client-settings/<rid>` — only the fields the admin TOUCHED, with `expected_version` and `base` (the stored values the page loaded) and `touched`; a billing change needs `billing_status_reason` and is recorded as `source='admin_override'` (leaving `paused` clears `paused_until` and `pause_reason`). A save that CHANGES a field in `step_up_fields` — `billing_status`, a module switch, `owner_email` — needs the step-up (403 `reauth_required`), asked after every other check. → `{ok, version, saved, changed}`; 400 `{unknown_fields}` or a sentence per problem (bounds, JSON settings, a bad timezone, the Place-ID/reviews rule); 409 `{conflict, fields, labels, current, current_version}` when a field this save changes was changed by someone else (the compare-and-swap 409 carries only `{conflict, fields:[], current_version}`).
- `POST /admin/api/client/<rid>/reviews-fetching` `{on}` → `{ok, reviews_live, via}`; 400 with the reason it cannot fetch.
- Brand: `GET /admin/api/brand/<rid>` → `{ok, restaurant_id, version, brand_*, category, exclude_from_learning, profile{}, learning}`; `POST` — changed fields plus `expected_version`, `clear:[field]` to empty one; colour `#rrggbb`, logo https only → the stored values; 409 `{conflict, current, current_version}`. Since the memory round (9/29/26) `learning` is `models.learning_status`: `{eligible, reason, label, automatic, override, since, learns_for_itself, billing_history}` — whether this account teaches Cavnar AI's pooled learning and why not (a demo, a test account by an admin's flag or automatically by its name, internal billing, the admin's own home), and (re-audit 9/29/26) whether it learns for itself (`models.learns_for_itself`: false only for a demo or an admin's exclusion) and whether it has a billing history. `POST` takes `learning_override` (`include` | `exclude` | `""` for automatic; anything else is 400) and an optional `learning_history`: a change TO `include` starts the account's teaching now (`learning_since`) unless its history counts — `learning_history: true`, or, when the save leaves it out, an account with a billing history (`models.learning_billing_history`, PLATFORM-8; the console sends the box as it stands, ticked by default for such an account); re-sending an `include` already stored does not move it.
- `POST /admin/upload-data/<rid>` (multipart `data_type`, `csv_file` or `csv_content`) runs the owner route's own body as the admin; no owner email or webhook where sending is not allowed; 413 over the row cap.
- `POST /admin/import-reviews/<rid>` (multipart `file`, `platform`: tripadvisor | doordash | ubereats) → `{ok, imported, new, platform, already_had?, skipped?, rejected?}`; an unknown platform is refused.
- Templates `GET` / `POST /admin/api/templates/<rid>`, `POST …/<tid>/delete`. Alert contacts `GET /admin/alert-contacts/<rid>`, `POST /admin/api/alert-contacts/<rid>` `{name, phone}` (at most 2, never with SMS consent — an operator typing a number is not that person consenting) → `{ok, id, name, phone}`, `POST …/<cid>/delete`. Staff notes `POST /admin/staff-notes/<rid>` (form fields) → `{ok, id, notes, appended}` — ADDS to the person's constraints; delete → `{ok, deleted}` for undo.
- Menu extraction (`POST /admin/upload-menu-pdf/<rid>`, `/admin/fetch-menu-from-url/<rid>`, `/admin/refresh-menu-notes/<rid>`) → `{ok, job_id, status:"pending"}` (409 one already running; 503 the pool is full); poll `GET /admin/api/menu-extract/<job_id>` — the text to review, nothing saved. `POST /admin/create-client` takes an optional `timezone` (one of `time_utils.COMMON_TIMEZONES`) → `{ok, restaurant_id, setup_job_id, timezone, timezone_defaulted, …}`; the Places menu fetch and the DocuSign send run as a job, `GET /admin/api/create-client/<job_id>` (a failed job answers 200 `{ok:false, status:"error"}`). Many create-client refusals still answer 200 `{ok:false, error}`.
- POS admin saves and disconnects (Toast, Square, Clover, RPOWER) and the RPOWER bootstrap are **step-up**; a save refuses a store already bound to another live restaurant (400 — the operator's message names that restaurant); demos exempt. `GET /admin/client-settings/<rid>` and `/admin/client-data/<rid>` (the legacy HTML pages) answer a support login 403.

**The console's data** (fix round C)
- `GET /admin/api/badges` → `{ok, counts{customer, platform, internal, attention, critical}, kpis{…}}` from the memo (up to 300 s old) — poll this, not the overview. Since the UI wave (9/29/26) Overview, Customers and Analytics read the paged lists and the badges — Analytics reads `/admin/api/clients/list?segment=customer&per_page=200` page by page — so the full `/admin/api/clients` records have no console caller (tests still use the route); the console no longer calls the owner route `/api/import-tripadvisor` (it posts to `/admin/import-reviews/<rid>`).
- `GET /admin/api/overview` — issue keys the integration wave added: `owed_sends`, `reconcile`, `contract:declined` / `contract:voided`, `ai_budget:*`, `ai_anomaly:*`, `backup` (`ai_cost` is gone), and since the memory round `learning:<curve>` ("Learning curve worsening: recommendation acceptance" — the latest learning scorecard month flags a curve worsening or flat) and `learning:fatigue` (most of what the owner settled went dismissed or ignored; Home, the report and the feed now show fewer), both warnings linking to the client's AI tab. Adds `issue_counts`, `issues_total`, `internal_issues`, `series{mrr, signups, accounts, source, note}`, `webhooks` and KPIs (`mrr_list/billed/committed/past_due/source/fallback_accounts/mismatches`, `covered_locations`, `paused`, `internal_accounts`, `ai_quality_24h`, `request_errors_24h`, `email_bounced/complained/delivered_today`, `stalled_reviews`, `deletion_requests`, `fetch_behind`, `trial_conversion`, `email_rates`, `api_window_label`). `GET /admin/api/issues` still returns only `generated_at, errors, unavailable` of the meta.
- `GET /admin/api/issues/list?segment=attention|customer|platform|internal|all&severity=&q=&restaurant_id=&category=&sort=severity|age|restaurant&page=&per_page=` (≤200) → `{ok, items, page, per_page, total, pages, counts, filters}`; `GET /admin/api/clients/list?q=&health=&segment=customer|internal|all&status=&sort=name|health|mrr|last_active|created&page=&per_page=` → slim rows and counts. Also `&churn=` (a churn-risk level), `&has_issues=1`, `&joined_days=` (created within N days) and `&inactive_days=` (no owner activity in N days, or never); a day count that isn't a whole number is ignored. Issues carry `occurrence_at`, `resolvable`, `reopened{…}`, `segment`, `category`, `action_kind` (post | link | mailto), `action_href`, `action_payload`.
- `POST /admin/api/issues/resolve` `{key, occurrence_at?, note?, undo?}` → `{ok, key, occurrence_at, scope: occurrence|condition, note}`; **409 `{resolvable:false}`** for `scheduler`, `platform:error_rate`, `backup` and deletion requests (and any issue the build marks `resolvable:false`); `GET /admin/api/issues/resolutions` → `{ok, resolved, total, history}`.
- `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>` adds `segment`, billing (`billed_by`, `covers`, `subscription`, `pause_reason`, `paused_until`, `days_in_trial`, `signed_at`), reviews `awaiting` (every unanswered review), `activity`, `on_ios`, `deletion`, `churn_risk.scored/since`, `data_health.stale`, integration `error_since` / `revoked_at` (an outbound webhook's `last_success` is its last SUCCESS, `webhooks.last_success_at`), `data_sources`, `ai_quality`, `logins[].label`, `timeline_url`, and since the integration wave `storm_cap` (`{cap, until, reason, …}` or null), `suppressions[].operator` and `issue_texts` (`{failed_7d, retrying, not_texted_open, last_failed_at, recent[]}` — the issue texts that failed, are backing off, or were not sent). `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/timeline?types=&limit=&before=` → `{ok, restaurant_id, types, events[{at, type, label, tone, detail}], next_before}`. `GET /admin/api/client/<rid>/billing/live` — the one Stripe read (502 on a Stripe error for that client only).
- `GET /admin/forms/availability.pdf?restaurant_id=&download=1` — the branded employee availability sheet (`print_forms.availability_sheet`, one letter page), blank or with that restaurant's name on it; inline to print, or an attachment with `download=1`; 404 for an unknown restaurant. Customers → Onboarding → Client forms (10/6/26).
- `GET /admin/api/onboarding` (in-service accounts still onboarding; each row carries `contract_status` and `signed_at`), `/admin/api/adoption?days=` (1–90), `/admin/api/queues` (guest campaign texts, delayed actions, alert holds, review requests, held DSR pushes, the push and webhook outboxes — `push_outbox` / `webhook_outbox`, queued and delivering pending, failed and expired failed — and owed billing email), `/admin/api/data-sources?segment=`, `/admin/api/business-metrics?days=`, `GET` / `POST /admin/api/vendor-costs` `{vendor, month: YYYY-MM, amount_usd, note}` (admins only; audited).
- `/admin/api/emails` adds `generated_at`, `rates` (`7d` / `30d`: `sent` attempted, `accepted` — only what Resend took: sent, delayed, delivered, bounced, complained — `failed`, `bounces`, `complaints`, `delivered`, `in_flight` (sent or delayed, no outcome yet), `bounce_rate` (2 decimals), `complaint_rate` (3 decimals), `enough`, `last_bounce_at`, `last_complaint_at`; one definition, `models.email_delivery_stats`), `resend_webhook`, `today.sent`, `suppressed_total`, `suppressions[].operator`, a zero-filled `daily`; `/admin/api/notifications` adds `generated_at`, `storm_counts`, `auto_caps` (from `alert_storm_caps`; `auto_caps_supported` is true wherever that table exists), `outboxes{push, webhooks}` (queued, in flight, failed), `issue_texts`, `alerts[].channels`, `posts_failed_total`, `engagement.delivered/opened`; `/admin/api/search` results carry `match`; recommendation analytics can return `outcome_rate: null` and `ras: null` with `ras_partial`.

**Jobs, the backup and manual runs** (fix round D)
- `GET /admin/api/jobs` adds `generated_at`, `grouped[]` with each failure group's `kind` (`job` / `request` / `ai_quality` / `audit` — counts are per job AND kind), `jobs[{job, label, cadence, what, sends, runnable, sla_minutes, stuck_after_minutes, lane, retry, state: ok|partial|failed|running|stuck|overdue|never, last_ok_at, avg_ms, runs_7d, failed_7d, partial_7d, running_since, overdue, history[14]}]`, `heartbeat{beat_age_minutes, loop_completed_age_minutes, running_job, running_minutes, running_bound_minutes, wedged, loop_stalled, stale, stale_after_minutes}`, `lease`, `backup`, `storage`, `requests`, `operator_alert{key, subject, sent, channels, error, created_at}`, `missed_windows`, `dsr_missing`, `jobs_overdue`, `local_sends_refused`, `local_sends_refused_reason`, `history_runs`, and since the memory round `retention: {refused: [{table, days, floor}], cap_rows} | null` (`ops.retention_refusals`: a retention window set under its floor right now — `prune_ledgers` deletes nothing from that table until its `RETAIN_*` variable is fixed; null when unreadable, never "none"); `failures[]` gain `restaurant_id` and `kind`. A `prune_ledgers` run's stored result keeps `refused` / `capped` (as many as fit the 500-character result; `<key>_n` carries the full count).
- `POST /admin/api/jobs/<job>/run` → 200 `{ok, job, context, queued:true, request_id, message}` (handed to the scheduler; a second press joins the pending request); 200 `{ok, job, context, queued:false}` on a laptop for a non-sending job; 409 `{ok:false, error}` already running, a sending job on a local backend (audited as refused), or the request could not be written; 404 unknown job. `GET /admin/api/jobs/<job>/runs?limit=` (≤200) → `{ok, job, spec, runs[{id, job, started_at, finished_at, duration_ms, ok, state, error, context, result_json, request_id, restaurant_id, manual}]}` — `job` a `jobs_registry.JOBS` name or one of the per-restaurant runs `review_fetch_one`, `pos_sync_one` and `event_sync_one` (an owner's follow or Put back re-sync); any other name is a 404. The catalog editor's re-sync (`event_catalog_resync`) runs on the admin job pool and writes no `job_runs` row — its outcome is the `event_catalog.resync` audit row.
- `POST /admin/fetch-reviews/<rid>` → 200 `{ok, job_id, joined, message}`; 409 a local backend or the scheduled fetch is running; 400 no platform id or Google connection; 404. `POST /admin/toast/sync/<rid>`, `/admin/square/sync/<rid>`, `/admin/clover/sync/<rid>` and `/admin/rpower/sync/<rid>` → `{ok, job_id, message}` (all four through `scheduler.start_manual_pos_sync` on the admin task pool). Poll `GET /admin/api/tasks/<job_id>` → `{ok, status: pending|done|error, result}` — admins only (403 for support), only the admin tasks (`review_fetch_one`, `pos_sync_one`; any other job id is a 404), and only the keys a poll reads (`ok, state, attempted, failed, skipped, hit_bound, provider, error, message`, the job's own counts under `result.result`).
- `GET /admin/api/backup` → `{ok, status (ops.backup_status: state ok|stale|no_offsite|failed|never, ages, sizes, last_error, offsite_targets, summary), configured{encryption_key, s3, email, any}, runs[≤30], storage{rows, growth_bytes_per_day, days_to_full}}`.

**Messaging** (fix round E)
