Since the memory round (9/29/26) both computed forecasts in the AI reads — the Reviews read's `forecast` (`review_rating_week`) and the Marketing read's (`marketing_reach_week`) — go through `forecast_log.shown`: null while the record reads "often wide" or no better than the naive forecasts, otherwise `predicted` is the raw figure corrected by the record's consistent lean, and the read's own line says so (the Reviews read's "Next week" line, the Marketing read's FORECAST line: "… already corrected because earlier forecasts here …"). The raw figure is what is recorded and scored, so a correction never feeds on itself. The payload's `forecast` object keeps its shape — no `withheld` or `note` key. Waste-trend notes read "N% trend strength · N weeks" (and carry `trend_strength_pct`) instead of "high/moderate confidence". The Reviews executive brief's `trend` adds `first, latest, change, weeks, reviews, min_reviews_per_week` — the web header renders this instead of its own rule. The morning brief's trend line and the negative-trend SMS/email read the same scorer (declining at medium/high confidence) and state weeks and reviews.

**AI visibility** — `GET /api/ai-visibility`, `/mobile/api/intel/ai-visibility`: `ai_score_band` (`often | sometimes | rarely | uncertain`), `ai_score_label`, `ai_score_tone` (`good | warn | bad | neutral`) — computed from the 90% range `ai_score_low`–`ai_score_high`, never the point; a range crossing a band edge reads "somewhere between 22% and 86% — too few questions to say more". `presence_label` ("Listing strength" — not GBP completeness), `presence_band_label`, `presence_tone` from one table (`client_api.PRESENCE_BANDS`: 80 strong/good, 60 a few gaps/warn, 40 needs work/warn, else critical gaps/bad). Ask `read_ai_visibility` adds `ai_score_low`, `ai_score_high`, `ai_score_label`, `answered_queries` and a `note` to quote the range. `business_intelligence.gather()['visibility']` adds `ai_score_low`, `ai_score_high`, `answered`. The drop alert and the intel × reviews link fire only when the two runs' ranges do not overlap (`notify.visibility_range`).

**Market average** — `GET /mobile/api/intel` adds `own_vs_market`, `standing` (`ahead | level | behind | null`), `standing_label`, `standing_tone` (`competitor_intel_format.market_standing`; compared only against Google's all-time rating, never the imported sample). Since Benchmarking #38: a standing needs `MARKET_MIN_MATCHED` (3) rated rivals matched on cuisine and price (or added by the owner), else `standing` is null with `standing_why_not`; rivals from the widened-radius fallback never enter the average (`market_excluded_widened`), one venue weighs at most `MARKET_WEIGHT_CAP_REVIEWS` (500) reviews (`market_effective_reviews`), `market_matched_n` and `market_radius_km` are sent, and "level" ("About level with the block", tone `neutral`) is a symmetric band inside one standard error of the gap (`standing_margin`, never under 0.1★), said in `standing_basis` ("3 restaurants matched on cuisine and price within 2.4 km · a gap inside ±0.1★ reads as level"). The web Intel page receives the same object as the template variable `intel_market` (`own_rating`, `own_rating_basis`, `own_rating_count`, `market_rating`, `market_rating_reviews`, `market_rating_n`, plus the standing fields). The welcome email's first look uses the same weighted average (`neighbourhood.reviews`, `basis`).

**Reviews read below its data floor** (NS4 C1) — `GET /api/review-insight` and `/mobile/api/reviews/insight` return 200 with `{insight (fixed copy), insufficient_data: true, reason, window_reviews, floor, recs: [], diagnoses: [], figures_verified/names_verified/causes_verified: true, stale: false}` and no model call when the restaurant has no reviews or fewer than `notify.MIN_TREND_REVIEWS_PER_WEEK` in the last 28 days; no `claim_kinds`, `trend`, `revenue_at_risk` or `benchmark`. The web renders it as an empty state ("Not enough reviews yet"); iOS shows the text. `revenue_at_risk` is `{available: false, reason}` for a restaurant in a multi-location group (the Luca elasticity is an independent-restaurant finding) and otherwise adds `source`, `source_year`; `benchmark` (competitors) needs 3 rated competitors. Food cost `pct` payloads (`cogs.build_food_cost_pct`) add `benchmark` (`{low, high, label, source, year, inferred, line}` or null) and `label` is null when neither a target nor a registry entry exists.

**Labor vs industry** — web `savings_breakdown` and `/mobile/api/labor` `savings_breakdown` add `labor_industry_pct` and `labor_industry_basis`: the published labor median for the restaurant's type from `benchmark_registry` (`thresholds.labor_industry_benchmark` — today 34.2, the NRA 2025 profitable full-service median, for full-service types), and **`null` for a type with no published entry** (NS4 H3), when both clients hide every "vs industry" mark and line. The "$ under industry" tiles are gone on both clients (Benchmarking #34): the published figure includes benefits and this labor % is wages from shifts, so `labor_vs_industry_monthly` / `_annual` are always **0** (re-audit #2 — sent only because shipped iOS decodes them as non-optional Doubles; nothing computes them). `thresholds.labor_industry_benchmark` reads the Benchmark Engine's `industry` comparison and carries its `comparable` / `definition_note`; `thresholds.labor_vs_industry_monthly` returns 0 unless `comparable=True`. Web `sales_lift_yr` is now always 0 (the unsourced 3.1% figure was retired).

**Holiday banner** — web `labor_upcoming[]` and `/mobile/api/labor` `labor_upcoming[]`: `{name, date (ISO), date_str (M/D/YY — was "September 21st"), days_away, lift_pct, based_on, label, claim_kind}`. `label` states what THIS restaurant's sales did on that holiday last year when known ("Last year 38% above a typical Thursday here — …"), else "Holiday — check your own history". Clients render `label` in place of the old generic copy.

**Value naming** — `GET value` (`/api/value`, `/mobile/api/value`) adds `sections: [{key: "measured", heading: "What was measured", figures: ["delivered"]}, {key: "surfaced", heading: "What Cavnar surfaced / still available", figures: ["avoided", "surfaced", "opportunity"]}]`; `delivered.scope = "monthly_rate"`. The Home `value` block (K4) adds `scope: "monthly_rate"` and `cumulative_scope: "measured_days_sum"`; `value_delivered.headline` adds `scope`. Savings milestones carry `data.scope = "all_time_sum"`.

**Inventory** — `analyse_inventory` adds `benchmark_state` (`measured | not_measured | no_data`; a week with nothing logged is "No waste recorded", tone `neutral`, never "Excellent"), `recoverable_kind: "opportunity"` and `annual_recoverable_basis` ("one week's count × 4.33 weeks a month × 12 — a projection of what is still being lost, not money saved"). Clients style recoverable as available, never as a win.

**Monthly review** — `review.prime_cost` is the CLOSED month measured (`{pct, previous, delta, claim_kind: "measured", window, basis}`), or `null` when either half cannot be measured — never this month's projection, so the screen and the email agree.

**Schedule** — likely-edit rows (`likely_edits[]` of kind `predicted`) are withheld until the restaurant's leave-one-out backtest covers 4 held-out drafts, and each carries `backtest_hit_rate`, `backtest_hits`, `backtest_flagged`, `backtest_weeks`, `base_rate`, `calibration_note` (also appended to `text`). `standby_days[]` add `base_rate` and `assumption` ("Assumes no-shows are independent…"); people with no clock-in record count at the base rate; every rate is smoothed toward it. Roster `reliability` entries add `no_shows`, `raw_no_show_rate`, `base_rate`, `no_show_threshold` (= `shift_quality.UNRELIABLE_RATE`, the engine's line — clients colour red at exactly this) and `unreliable`; `no_show_rate` is now the smoothed rate.

**Demand signals** — an event with no covers and no lift is stored with `lift_pct: null`; `by_date` takes the event's MEASURED lift here once it has `event_memory.EFFECT_MIN_N` nights (`lift_source: "measured"`, `measured_n`; memory round 9/29/26 — a fill-a-night campaign reads the one campaign measurement), else marks it `assumed: true` with `assumed_lift_pct` (25, said as an assumption), which never raises a demand level. Every event with a record carries it in `measured: [{label, median_lift_pct, n, last, applies, owner_lift_pct}]`, so an owner's own figure is said beside what was measured. `demand.slow_days()` entries carry `consistency` (share of that weekday's nights under the typical day; "reliably slow" needs 0.75).

**DSR** — the reviews block's `avg_rating` is `null` under 5 reviews, with `detail.rating_note` ("3 reviews — not rated (a rating needs 5)") and `detail.rating_min_reviews`. The food block's `est_food_cost_pct` is `null` under 50% of units costed, with `estimate.coverage_floor_pct` and `estimate.coverage_note`; the label speaks of units, not dishes. The weekly .xlsx marks provisional nights "(provisional)" and adds a footnote.

**Morning brief** — the money line adds `money: {low, high, per: "month", label, claim_kind, basis}` (a point figure has low == high) so no client regex-collapses a range.

**Money kinds (workstream M, 9/24/26; NS3 financial audit).** Every money figure carries its kind — `measured | estimate | projection | opportunity | plan | forecast | price` (plus `benchmark` for a comparison to an industry figure) — from where it is computed, so a client labels it and never calls an opportunity, projection or budget "saved". Renamed or added fields:
- **Labor** — `labor.analyse_shifts` adds `costed_labor` (labor on the days with a sales figure: the figure that pairs with `total_sales`; `total_labor_cost` stays, every day), `data_days` (days with shifts AND sales — what the projection floor counts: `period_too_short_to_project` is now true under `MIN_DAYS_TO_EXTRAPOLATE` data days, not calendar days) and `money_kinds` (`{total_sales: measured, total_labor_cost|costed_labor|overtime_premium: estimate, potential_savings*: opportunity}`). The web `savings_breakdown` and `/mobile/api/labor` `savings_breakdown` are one function, `labor.savings_breakdown`: on the sample week every dollar is **0** and `dollars_withheld: "sample"` (0, not null — shipped iOS decodes them non-optional); `labor_overtime` is `analysis.overtime_premium` on both (the web re-priced at the flat rate: $351 vs $306); it adds `kinds` (`labor_monthly: opportunity, labor_annual: projection, labor_overtime: estimate, labor_vs_industry_*: benchmark`), `overtime_period_days`, `data_days` (web: `labor_kinds`, `labor_overtime_period_days`, `labor_dollars_withheld`). `/mobile/api/labor` adds `costed_labor`, `total_sales` (null on sample), `data_days`, `money_kinds`. `GET /api/labor-gap` and `/mobile/api/labor/gap`: `monthly_gap` now equals `potential_savings_monthly` (0 when not over target), scaled at `metrics.DAYS_PER_MONTH`, with `kind: "opportunity"`; the mobile route is gated on sample data like the web one. Draft-schedule overtime (`projected_cost`, `overtime_forecast[].overtime_line`, a move's `saves`) is priced from `labor.OVERTIME_THRESHOLD_HOURS` (40), never the owner's weekly hours ceiling (a cap), and a move's text reads "That move would save about $X".
- **Food** — each cost driver carries `value_kind` (`waste|menu|sourcing` → opportunity, `price|portion` → estimate); `cost_drivers` adds `totals_by_kind` (the de-duplicated total split by kind; they sum to `total_monthly_deduplicated`) and `total_kinds`, and `total_basis` names the mix; the food brief's `money_involved` adds `totals_by_kind`. Every driver is scaled at `metrics.WEEKS_PER_MONTH` / `DAYS_PER_MONTH`. Home's food "At stake" is `food_cost_intelligence.deduplicated_total` (it read $750 where the card read $400). Home's food snapshot tile adds `kind` (`measured` for the food-cost %, `opportunity` for recoverable) and its unit reads `est. recoverable / mo`; the "At stake" secondary carries `kind: "opportunity"`. `/mobile/api/home` inventory KPI sublabel is `est. recoverable / mo` with `kind`; the pulse label is `est. recoverable`. The Ask `read_food` tool adds `projection_basis`, `money_periods` and `money_kinds`. `waste_trend` stats add `money_kinds` (`savings_if_at_target: opportunity` — never a "good" tone).
- **DSR** — the food block's `recoverable_monthly` is now inventory's figure (waste above tolerance, one week projected; `detail.recoverable.kind: "opportunity"`) and the cost-driver total is the new `drivers_at_stake_monthly` (`detail.drivers_at_stake` with `totals_by_kind`). The narrative's model slot `largest_money_saving` is replaced by `largest_opportunity`; `largest_money_saving` stays in every narrative as `null` (stored ones too, via `dsr.access.narrative_for`). `verification` adds `opportunity`, `plan` and `by_kind: {measured, estimate, opportunity, plan}`; `measured` now counts only lines resting on measured facts. Week and period `totals`: `budget_net`, `budget_gross` and `last_year_net` cover the SAME measured days as `net` (so `vs_X == net − X`); the whole range's figures are `budget_net_full_range`, `budget_gross_full_range`, `last_year_net_full_range` (+ `_days`), and a measured night with no budget withholds the total and its comparison with `budget_net_note`.
- **Value** — `value_delivered.avoided`'s content line is priced per piece (`AGENCY_PER_PIECE`, $125 of the $1,500 month, never more than a month's fee in one month) and adds `pieces`; `rates()` adds `agency_per_piece`, `agency_pieces_per_month`. `ledger_lines` says "alerts sent to you" (not "before they became problems"); the iOS Home receipt says "sent to you this week".
- **Billing** — `GET /api/billing-info` and `/mobile/api/account/billing` `amount` takes its suffix from the Stripe price's interval (`$11,990/yr`, not `/mo`) and adds `interval` (`pricing.billing_amount_label`).
- **Sales audit** — `roi` is the cost opportunity in the RECOMMENDED LIVE modules only (`roi.numerator`, `roi.categories`, `roi.basis`, `kind: "opportunity"`), `null` when none is recommended or the restaurant is in its opening months; `plan.monthly_equiv` is `round(annual / 12)` ($291 / $541 / $999).

## People facts for the schedule — additive fields (schedule audit fix round 10/3/26, F2)

Each route below exists twice (web `/api/…`, mobile `/mobile/api/…`) through `strategy_routes._ROUTES` or the client_api → mobile_api delegate, unless it is a staff-app route.

- **`GET labor/roster`** — every entry adds `recent_roles` (every role worked in their last eight weeks, most worked first; `role` is now the one worked most, D-17), `dormant` (bool — no shift in `staff_settings.DORMANT_WEEKS` (6) weeks and nothing on file saying they are still here, E-3; never whoever runs the floor, or a manager of any kind), `last_worked_label` (M/D/YY), `dormant_text` (`"Not worked since 8/14/26 — deactivate?"`, or null), `role_scores` (`{"Bartender": 4}` — per-role Operational Scores, D-12), `rated_label`, `rating_due` (bool — the newest counted rating is 90+ days old) and `rating_due_text` (`"Rated 6/1/26 — still right?"`). Marking a dormant person still here is any `POST labor/staff-settings` for them (e.g. `{employee_name, active: true}`): it keeps them off the dormant list for six more weeks.
- **`GET labor/team`** (web `/api/labor/team` → `mobile_labor_team`) — the list now reads the one roster (`staff_settings.roster`: one entry per person, the role worked most) and each entry adds the same `dormant`, `last_worked_label`, `dormant_text`, `role_scores`, `rated_label`, `rating_due`, `rating_due_text`; `coverage` adds `due_for_rerate` and `due_for_rerate_names`.
- **`POST labor/team/rating`** — takes `role` (`{"employee_name", "role": "Server AM", "score": 4}`): a score for that role, kept per role family (`attribute: "role:server"` in the answer); without it, the overall score as before. `attribute: "role:<role>"` is accepted too.
- **`GET/POST account/targets`** — `targets.salaried` entries add `matched` (the roster spelling the entry means, or null), `suggestion` and `warning`; `targets.salaried_warnings: [{name, suggestion, text}]` names a salaried entry that matches nobody on the roster (`"Gabriel Huerta matches nobody on your roster, so the schedule can't place them and a punch under another spelling is paid by the hour — is it Gabe Huerta?"`); both owner-only like `salaried`. `salaried_add` saves the entry under the spelling of the one person its name means (an alias counts) with their `person_id` (D-7).
- **`POST /staff/api/time-off`** (staff app) — takes `start_time`, `end_time` (any time `"4pm"`, `"16:00"`, `"4:00pm"`) or `daypart` (`lunch`/`morning` or `dinner`/`night`) for part of each day (D-39); 400 `"Give a time or lunch/dinner, not both."` / `"The end is before the start."` / `"That isn't a time — try 4:00pm."`. The row answered (and `GET /staff/api/time-off` rows) carry `start_time`, `end_time`, `daypart`; `GET` rows add `span_label` (`"10/7/26, until 4:00pm"`), and `still_scheduled` lists only the shifts that reach into that part of the day.
- **`GET labor/time-off`** — each request adds `start_time`, `end_time`, `daypart` and `span_label` (`"10/7/26, until 4:00pm"`); `POST labor/time-off/<id>/decide` names only the published shifts inside that part of the day (`conflicts`, `warning`).
- **`GET/POST labor/rules`** — the rules add `full_time_min_hours` (default 30; 0 turns it off, 0–60): a full-timer's minimum when the owner set none for them (D-41).
- **The generation review** (`result.review`) adds `unmatched_names: [{source, name, detail, suggestion?}]` — a fact on file (time off, availability, a note, a station skill, a salaried entry, a closer flag) whose name matches nobody on the roster, with a line in `review.lines`; lines for people left off the draft as dormant (`result.dormant: {name: last worked iso}`) and for regulars with no shift (`result.regulars_not_scheduled: [{name, usual_hours}]`). `result.role_scores` and `result.prior_pattern` (each person's usual week: `days`, `dayparts`, `avg_hours`, `hours_weeks`, `starts`) ride along.

## Additive fields for the web setup screens (schedule fix round 10/3/26, UI W2)

Both twins share each body; every field is additive and optional to a client.

- `GET labor/rules` — `salaried_caps` [{name, cap, own}] for the account holder only: each salaried person on the roster and the weekly hours code schedules them for (their own maximum when `own`, else the salaried cap).
- `GET /account/memory` — `schedule_checks` {fact id: {checked, reads_as, text}} beside each of the account holders' staffing rules (a constraint or preference about labor or the schedule) the login may read, as every draft checks it; `{}` on an archive page or for a login without labor.
- `POST /account/memory/add` and `schedule_setup.owner_rule_preview` — a rule about two people ("keep Ana and Bo apart") reads back as checked ("Ana and Bo kept apart"); a pairing on some days only stays unchecked.
- `GET /issues` — each coverage issue carries `cover_gaps` [{employee, role, shift_start, status, covered_by, asked, cover: {name, kind stay|off, how, for, shift_start} | null}] (`issues.cover_gaps`); on an issue about more than one person `askable_covers` (what Home and /i/<token> present) is one cover per open gap, else up to two as before. The /i/<token> page lists each gap with one "Ask … to stay on for / to cover …'s 5:00pm" button.
- `GET /people/<key>` — `choices.certification_labels`; `attendance` adds `called_out`, `late_shifts`, `late_rate` (None below the floor of clocked shifts).
- `GET labor/team` — team entries add `recent_roles`; the payload adds `strength_crews` {role: {target, crew, per_person, source, known}} (`schedule_setup.strength_crews_view`, the scorer's own reading); `POST labor/team/thresholds` returns it for the saved targets.
- `GET labor/profiles` — profiles are built with the calibration's tuning (`tuned` on a built-in it changed) and the payload adds `critical_floors` (the default floor per critical dimension, stations included).
